is uniquely determined by the map
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(A)\rightarrow A
, so the binomial ring structure is unique.

Let now
A
be a binomially closed torsion-free ring. We can restrict the map
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(A)\otimes\mathbb Q\rightarrow A\otimes\mathbb Q
to
\mathrm{Bin}(A)\subset\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(A)\otimes\mathbb Q
. Again, identifying
\mathrm{Bin}(A)\subset\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(A)\otimes\mathbb Q
with the
\mathbb Z
-subalgebra generated by
\binom{[a]}{n}
, we get that the resulting map
\mathrm{Bin}(A)\rightarrow A\otimes\mathbb Q
in fact factors through
A
. To show that the resulting map
\mathrm{Bin}(A)\rightarrow A
defines a module structure over
\mathrm{Bin}

we need to check that the two compositions
\mathrm{Bin}(\mathrm{Bin}(A))\rightarrow A
are the same. Since both
\mathbb Z
-modules are torsion-free this enough to check after tensoring with
\mathbb Q
, where via
2.28
these maps can be identified with the analogous compositions
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(A))\rightarrow A
tensor
\mathbb Q
. Thus the map
\mathrm{Bin}(A)\rightarrow A
above defines a binomial ring structure on
A
.

∎

Remark 2.30
.

The proof of
2.29
also shows that the forgetful functor
\BinAlg\rightarrow\CAlg
is a fully faithful embedding. One can describe the left adjoint explicitly as
binomial closure

L_{\mathrm{Bin}}
; for a commutative algebra
A
,
L_{\mathrm{Bin}}A
is given by the
\mathbb Z
-subalgebra
L_{\mathrm{Bin}}(A)\subset A\otimes\mathbb Q
generated by the binomial coefficients
\binom{a}{n}\in A\otimes\mathbb Q
.

Remark 2.31
.

2.29
tells us in particular that the binomial ring structure on a commutative ring
A
is a condition and not a structure: the data of a binomial ring is a commutative ring
A
whose underlying abelian group is torsion-free and which is binomially closed. Note that this is exactly how binomial rings were introduced in
[
Ell06
]
.

Let us now give some natural examples of binomial rings.

Example 2.32
.

1.

Ring of integers
\mathbb Z
is torsion-free and binomially closed, so by the above it has a unique binomial ring structure. The map

\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\mathbb Z,

corresponding to this module structure, is explicitly given by the ring homomorphism
\ev_{1}\colon\mathbb Z[\binom{x}{n}]_{n\geq 1}\rightarrow\mathbb Z
, which sends
x
to
1\in\mathbb Z
(here we identified
x
with the class
[1]\in\mathbb Z\subset\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq 1}(\mathbb Z)
). Note that this map sends
x\choose n
for
n\geq 2
to 0.

2.

More generally, let
S
be a set; then the ring
\Map_{{\mathrm{Set}}}(S,\mathbb Z)
of
\mathbb Z
-valued functions on
S
is torsion-free and binomially closed, and so has a unique binomial algebra structure.

3.

Any
\mathbb Q
-algebra
A
is a binomial ring.

4.

Any torsion-free
\lambda
-ring
A
whose Adams operations are trivial is a binomial ring
[
Ell06
]
, and in fact any classical binomial ring comes this way.

2.3
Polynomial functors and their nonabelian derived extensions

In this section we extend the binomial monad further to the full derived category
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
following
[
Rak20
, Section 4]
and
[
BM19
, Section 3]
(which in turn go back
[
Ill72
]
).

For a small
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}^{0}
we denote by
\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{C}^{0})
the full subcategory of the category
\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{C}^{0})\coloneqq\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}((\mathscr{C}^{0})^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
spanned by functors that preserve finite products (
[
Lur09
, Definiton 5.5.8.8]
). We will call
\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{C}^{0})
the
sifted completion of
\mathscr{C}^{0}
. In the case
\mathscr{C}^{0}=\mathrm{Lat}
the sifted completion
\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathrm{Lat})
is naturally identified with the category
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\subset{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
of connective objects (see e.g.
[
CS19
, Example 5.1.6(2)]
). Category
\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{C}^{0})
satisfies the following universal property:

Proposition 2.33

(
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.5.8.15]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{D}
be a
\infty
-category which admits all sifted colimits and let
\mathscr{C}\coloneqq\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{C}^{0})
be the sifted completion of a small
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}^{0}
. Let
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}_{\Sigma}
denote the
\infty
-category of functors that commute with sifted colimits. Then the restriction induces an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D})\rightarrow\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathscr{C}^{0},\mathscr{D})

with the inverse functor given by the left Kan extension along
\mathscr{C}^{0}\subset\mathscr{C}

If
F\colon\mathrm{Lat}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
is a functor, we will denote the corresponding sifted colimit preserving functor
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
by
\mathbf{L}F
and call it the
non-abelian (left) derived functor
of
F
.

Remark 2.34
.

By plugging
\mathscr{C}^{0}=\mathrm{Lat}
and
\mathscr{D}={\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
from
2.33
we get a monoidal equivalence of categories

\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}_{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{Lat}}({{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}^{\leq 0})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}(\mathrm{Lat}),

where
\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}_{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{Lat}}({{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}^{\leq 0})\subset\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}_{\Sigma}({{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}^{\leq 0})
denotes the full subcategory of endofunctors that preserve
\mathrm{Lat}\subset{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}^{\leq 0}
.
This way any filtered monad
\mathrm{T}^{\leq*}
on
\mathrm{Lat}
gives rise to a filtered monad
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{T}^{\leq*}
on
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
.

As noted by Brantner, if a functor
F
is polynomial (in the sense of
2.35
below), it can be naturally extended further to the whole derived category
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
. Let us briefly recall the relevant setup, following
[
Rak20
, Section 4.2]
. The following definition is a categorified version of
2.1
.

Definition 2.35

(Additively polynomial functors)
.

Let
\mathscr{A}
and
\mathscr{B}
be additive
\infty
-categories and assume
\mathscr{B}
is idempotent-complete. For a functor
F\colon\mathscr{A}\rightarrow\mathscr{B}
and an object
X\in\mathscr{A}
the difference functor
D_{X}F
is defined as

D_{X}F(Y):=\mathrm{fib}(F(X\oplus Y)\to F(Y)).

Then
F
is

1)

of degree 0
if
F
is a constant functor;

2)

of degree
n
if
D_{X}F
is of degree
n-1
for any object
X\in\mathscr{A}
;

3)

additively polynomial
if it is of degree
n
for some
n\geq 0.

Remark 2.36
.

If functors
F,G\colon\mathscr{A}\rightarrow\mathscr{B}
are of degrees
n
and
m
correspondingly, their direct sum
F\oplus G
is additively polynomial of degree
\max(n,m)
. If
\mathscr{B}
is stable or abelian this applies more generally for any extension of
G
by
F
in the category of functors
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})
. Also, given
F\colon\mathscr{A}\rightarrow\mathscr{B}
and
G\colon\mathscr{B}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
the composition
G\circ F
is again additively polynomial of degree
mn
.

Example 2.37
.

Functors
\otimes^{n}
,
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}^{n}
,
\wedge_{\mathbb Z}^{n}
,
\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{n}
on
\mathrm{Lat}
are additively polynomial of degree
n
.

Lemma 2.38
.

The functor

\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}\colon\mathrm{Lat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Lat}

is additively polynomial of degree
n
.

Proof.

By
2.24
the functor
\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}
has a finite filtration with the associated graded terms given by
\Gamma^{i}_{\mathbb Z}
. Since
\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{i}
is additively polynomial of degree
i
, we get that
\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}
is polynomial of degree
n
.
∎

Definition 2.39
.

For
n\geq 0
let
\mathbf{P}_{n}
denote the poset of subsets of the set
\{0,1,\ldots,n\}
. Let
\mathbf{P}_{n}^{\leq m}
(resp.
\mathbf{P}_{n}^{\geq m}
) denote the subcategory of
\mathbf{P}_{n}
spanned by subsets of cardinality at most (resp. at least)
m
. An
n
-cube in an
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
is a diagram
\chi\colon\mathbf{P}_{n}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
. An
n
-cube is called:

•

coCartesian
if
\chi
is a colimit diagram: namely, the natural map

\colim_{I\in\mathbf{P}_{n}^{\geq n}}\chi(I)\rightarrow\chi(\{0,\ldots,n\})

is an equivalence;

•

Cartesian
if
\chi
is a limit diagram: namely, the natural map

\chi(\varnothing)\rightarrow\lim_{I\in\mathbf{P}_{n}^{\geq 1}}\chi(I)

is an equivalence;

•

strongly coCartesian
if it is left Kan extended from from its restriction to
\mathbf{P}_{n}^{\geq 1}
.

Remark 2.40
.

If
\mathscr{C}
is stable then any
n
-cube is Cartesian if and only if it is coCartesian.

Definition 2.41

(Excisively polynomial functors)
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-category admitting finite colimits and let
\mathscr{D}
be stable. Then a functor
F
is called
n
-excisive
if it sends strongly coCartesian
n
-cubes to Cartesian
n
-cubes. A functor
F
is called
excisively polynomial
if it is
n
-excisive for some
n
.

The relation between the two notions of polynomial functors is given by the following:

Proposition 2.42

(
[
BM19
, Proposition 3.34]
,
[
JM99
, Proposition 5.10]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{C}\coloneqq\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{C}^{0})
be the sifted completion of the subcategory
\mathscr{C}^{0}\subset\mathscr{C}
of its compact projective objects and let
\mathscr{D}
be an
\infty
-category admitting small colimits. Let
\mathrm{F}\colon\mathscr{C}^{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
be an additively polynomial functor of degree
n
. Then the left derived functor
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{F}\colon\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
(see
2.33
) is
n
-excisive.

This applies in particular to
\mathscr{C}\coloneqq{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\simeq\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathrm{Lat})
.

Assume now that
\mathscr{C}
is stable, and that it has a
t
-structure given by
\mathscr{C}^{\leq 0}\subset\mathscr{C}
. Excisively polynomial functors are often fully determined by their restriction to
\mathscr{C}^{\leq 0}
. For brevity we will formulate the precise statement only in the case
\mathscr{C}\coloneqq{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
, and only for sifted colimit commuting functors.

Proposition 2.43
.

Let
\mathscr{D}
be an
\infty
-category that has small colimits and let

\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}_{\mathrm{e-poly}}^{\Sigma}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z),\mathscr{D})\subset\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z),\mathscr{D})

denote the full subcategory spanned by excisively polynomial functors that commute with sifted colimits. Then the restriction to
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\subset{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
induces an equivalence

\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}_{\mathrm{e-poly}}^{\Sigma}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z),\mathscr{D})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}_{\mathrm{e-poly}}^{\Sigma}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0},\mathscr{D}).

Proof.

This is
[
Rak20
, Proposition 4.2.15]
for
\mathscr{C}\coloneqq{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
and the
t
-structure defined by
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\subset{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
.
∎

Remark 2.44
.

The composition of an
n
-excisive and an
m
-excisive functor is
nm
-excisive, so excisively polynomial functors are closed under composition. In particular,
\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}_{\mathrm{e-poly}}(\mathscr{D})\subset\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}(\mathscr{D})
and, more generally,
\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}_{\mathrm{e-poly}}^{\Sigma}(\mathscr{D})\subset\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}_{\mathrm{e-poly}}(\mathscr{D})
have natural monoidal structures induced by composition.

Remark 2.45
.

The constant functor
\underline{X}\colon{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
for
X\in\mathscr{D}
is
0
-excisive, and is the restriction of the constant functor
\underline{X}\colon{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
. Thus, constant functors go to constant functors under the above equivalence. Also, in the case
\mathscr{D}={\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
, the identity functor
{\mathrm{id}}_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}
is 1-excisive and restricts to the identity functor
{\mathrm{id}}_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}}
(composed with the embedding
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
).

Construction 2.46

(Derived monad associated to a polynomial monad)
.

Let
{\mathrm{Poly}}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))\subset\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
denote the full subcategory spanned by additively polynomial functors. By
2.34
and
2.42
the operation of taking the
“left derived functor”
induces a monoidal functor

{\mathrm{Poly}}(\mathrm{Lat})\xrightarrow{\mathbf{L}(-)}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}_{\mathrm{e-poly}}^{\Sigma}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}).

Using the equivalence in
2.43
we then further get a monoidal functor

{\mathrm{Poly}}(\mathrm{Lat})\xrightarrow{\mathbf{L}(-)}\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}_{\mathrm{e-poly}}^{\Sigma}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)).

In particular, having a filtered monad
\mathrm{T}^{\leq*}\colon\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}(\mathrm{Lat})
with the property that each endofunctor
\mathrm{T}^{\leq n}
is in fact additively polynomial, we obtain a filtered monad

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{T}^{\leq*}\colon\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)),

which we will call the
filtered (full) derived monad
associated to
\mathrm{T}^{\leq*}
.

Construction 2.47
.

By
[
Rak20
, Proposition 4.1.4]
, if we have a filtered monad
\mathrm{T}^{\leq*}
on an
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
that admits small colimits, the corresponding colimit endofunctor
\mathrm{T}\coloneqq\colim_{n\in\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}}\mathrm{T}^{\leq n}\in\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}(\mathscr{C})
has a natural structure of a monad on
\mathscr{C}
.

Note that by
2.38
the filtered monad
\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq*}\colon\mathbb Z^{\times}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}(\mathrm{Lat})
takes values in polynomial functors and thus fits into the framework of
2.46
.

Definition 2.48

(Derived binomial monad)
.

The
filtered derived binomial monad

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq*}
on
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
is defined as the full filtered derived monad associated to the filtered monad
\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq*}
on
\mathrm{Lat}
. The
derived binomial monad

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
on
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
is defined as the colimit of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq*}
(see
2.47
).

Let us list some first properties of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
, as well as recipes of how to compute it.

Remark 2.49

(
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
restricted to
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
)
.

By construction, the restrictions of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
and
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}
to
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
are obtained as the left Kan extensions of
\mathrm{Bin}
and
\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}
from
\mathrm{Lat}\subset{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
. In particular, their restrictions to
\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{tf}}\subset{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
coincide with the functors
\mathrm{Bin}
and
\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}
from
Section

2.2
. For an arbitrary
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
, realizing it as the geometric realization
M\simeq|M_{\bullet}|
of a simplicial object
M_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{tf}})
,
\mathrm{Bin}(M)
is computed as the geometric realization
|\mathrm{Bin}(M_{\bullet})|
.

Remark 2.50

(Associated graded of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
)
.

Recall that by
2.24
the finite filtration on
\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}\in\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}(\mathrm{Lat})
given by
\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq*}
has the associated graded
\oplus_{k=0}^{n}\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{k}
. This way, passing to the colimit, we get a description of the associated graded for the filtration on
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
as
\oplus_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{L}\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{n}
(with
\gr_{n}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin})\simeq\mathbf{L}\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{n}
). Here
\mathbf{L}\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{n}
denotes the non-abelian derived functor of
\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{n}
.

Example 2.51
.

Let us describe
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}
in low degrees.

1.

\mathrm{Bin}^{0}\coloneqq\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq 0}\colon\mathrm{Lat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Lat}
was given by the constant functor
\underline{\mathbb Z}
, thus we get that
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq 0}\simeq\underline{\mathbb Z}
is the constant functor.

2.

By
2.18
(
2
)
\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}
splits as
\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq 0}\oplus\overline{\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}}
, which gives a splitting
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}\simeq\underline{\mathbb Z}\oplus\mathbf{L}\overline{\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}}
. The second summand
\mathbf{L}\overline{\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}}
has a finite filtration
\mathbf{L}\overline{\mathrm{Bin}}^{\leq*}
with the associated graded
\oplus_{i>0}^{n}\mathbf{L}\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{i}
.

3.

\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq 1}\colon\mathrm{Lat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Lat}
splits as
\underline{\mathbb Z}\oplus{\mathrm{id}}_{\mathrm{Lat}}
(
2.18
(
2
)), where
{\mathrm{id}}_{\mathrm{Lat}}\in\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}(\mathrm{Lat})
is the identity functor. Thus so is its non-abelian derived extension:
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq 1}\simeq\underline{\mathbb Z}\oplus{\mathrm{id}}_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}
.

Below, we will mostly be interested in values of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)
for
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\geq 0}
. By construction,
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
commutes with sifted colimits, but for the latter it would be more useful to also know that
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
commutes with some limits.

Let
X^{\bullet}\colon\Delta\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
be a cosimplicial object in a complete
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
. Recall that
X^{\bullet}
is called
m
-skeletal
if it is the right Kan extension of its restriction to
\Delta_{\leq m}\subset\Delta
. A
finite totalization
is a homotopy limit of a cosimplicial object that is
m
-skeletal for some
m
. A functor
F
is said to commute with finite totalizations if for any
X^{\bullet}
, that is
m
-skeletal for some
m
, the natural map

F(\Tot X^{\bullet})\rightarrow\Tot(F(X^{\bullet}))

is an equivalence.

Lemma 2.52
.

For any
n
the functor
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}\colon{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
commutes with finite totalizations.

Proof.

This follows from
[
BM19
, Proposition 3.37]
, since
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}
is
n
-excisive.
∎

We now can give a full recipe of how to compute
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
in general.

Remark 2.53

(Computing the value of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)
)
.

Let
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
; we have
M=\colim_{n}\tau^{\leq n}M
. Since
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
commutes with filtered colimits, we have
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)\simeq\colim_{n}\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\tau^{\leq n}M)
, so we can assume
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq m}
for some
m
. By definition,
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}=\colim_{\leq n}\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}
, and so it is enough to compute
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(M)
for all
n
. Any
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq m}
can be realized as the totalization
\Tot(M^{\bullet})
of an
m
-skeletal cosimplicial object
M^{\bullet}\in\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\Delta,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})
.
2.52
then says that
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(M)\simeq\Tot(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(M^{\bullet}))
. Finally, each
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(M^{i})
for
M^{i}\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
can be computed via
2.49
.

2.4
Derived binomial rings

We are now ready to define the main objects of study of this paper:

Definition 2.54
.

A
derived binomial ring

B
is a module over the monad
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
on
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
. The
\infty
-category of derived binomial rings will be denoted
\DBinAlg
.

Remark 2.55
.

By general properties of the category of modules over a monad,
\DBinAlg
is presentable and, consequently, has all small limits and colimits (
[
Rak20
, Proposition 4.1.10]
).
One has the forgetful functor

U\colon\DBinAlg\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)

which is conservative and has a left adjoint given by the
“free derived binomial ring functor”

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}\colon{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\DBinAlg.

Functor
{U}
commutes with all limits and sifted colimits, see
[
Lur17
, Corollary 4.2.3.5]
.

Remark 2.56

(Initial derived binomial ring)
.

Note that we have an isomorphism
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(0)\simeq\mathrm{Bin}(0)\simeq\mathbb Z
.
Note also that
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(0)\in\DBinAlg
is the initial object: indeed, by adjunction

\Map_{\DBinAlg}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(0),B)\simeq\Map_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}(0,{U}(B))\simeq\{*\}.

The map
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq 0}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}
in this case is an equivalence for any
n\geq 0
: indeed, the cofiber of this map has a finite filtration with the associated graded pieces given by
\mathbf{L}\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{k}(0)\simeq\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{k}(0)=0
. This way the canonical map

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(0)\rightarrow B

for any derived binomial ring
B
on the level of underlying complexes can be identified with the composition

\mathbb Z\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq 0}(B)\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(B)\rightarrow B.

Remark 2.57
.

Note that the filtered monad
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq*}
(resp. the colimit monad
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
) preserves the subcategory
\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{tf}}\subset{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
and its restriction to
\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{tf}}
by construction coincides with the filtered monad
\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq*}
(resp. its colimit
\mathrm{Bin}
) that we considered in
Section

2.2
. This induces a fully faithful embedding
\BinAlg\rightarrow\DBinAlg
of the category of classical binomial algebras into the category of derived binomial algebras, and the essential image is given by derived binomial algebras whose underlying complex lies in
\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{tf}}
.

The following will be the main example of a derived binomial ring considered in this paper:

Example 2.58

(Derived binomial ring structure on singular cohomology
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)
)
.

Let
X\in\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}
be a space and consider the binomial algebra

\mathbb Z^{X}\coloneqq\holim_{X}\mathbb Z,

where the limit is taken in
\DBinAlg
and
\mathbb Z
is the initial derived binomial ring.
Since the forgetful functor to
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
commutes with limits we have that the underlying complex of
\mathbb Z^{X}
is given by
\holim_{X}\mathbb Z\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
, which computes the singular cohomology
C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(X,\mathbb Z)
. Thus
\mathbb Z^{X}
gives a natural derived binomial ring structure on
C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(X,\mathbb Z)
for any space
X
. We will still denote this derived binomial ring by
C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(X,\mathbb Z)
, hopefully not leading to any confusion. Note that
C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(\mathrm{pt},\mathbb Z)\simeq\mathbb Z^{\mathrm{pt}}\simeq\mathbb Z
is the initial binomial ring.

Construction 2.59

(Underlying derived commutative ring)
.

In
[
Rak20
]
Raksit constructs a monad
12
12

12

Raksit’s construction is much more general, and here we specialized just to the case
\mathscr{C}={\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
.

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}
on
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
and defines the
\infty
-category
\DAlg(\mathbb Z)
of
derived commutative algebras
in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
as the category of modules over
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}
. This monad is obtained by the same procedure as in
2.46
but applied to the filtered monad
\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq*}_{\mathbb Z}
on
\mathrm{Lat}
. The map of filtered monads
\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq*}_{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq*}
on
\mathrm{Lat}
(
2.20
(3)) then induces a map

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq*}_{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq*}

of filtered monads on
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
, as well as a map
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
between their colimits. This induces a natural functor

\DBinAlg\rightarrow\DAlg(\mathbb Z),

that associates to a derived binomial ring its underlying derived commutative
\mathbb Z
-algebra. This functor is conservative and commutes with sifted colimits (since its composition with the forgetful functor
\DAlg(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
is the forgetful functor
{U}\colon\DBinAlg\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
).

Remark 2.60

(Underlying
\mathbb E_{\infty}
-algebra)
.

Let
\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z)
denote the category of
\mathbb E_{\infty}
-algebras in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
and let
\mathrm{T}\coloneqq\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z,\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!\!\in\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
be the monad given by taking (the underlying complex of) the free
\mathbb E_{\infty}
–algebra; we have an equivalence
\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z)\simeq\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}_{\mathrm{T}}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
. In
[
Rak20
]
Raksit shows that
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z,\mathbb E_{\infty}}
comes from a certain sifted colimit preserving filtered monad
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z,\mathbb E_{\infty}}^{\leq*}
on
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
via the colimit procedure and constructs a natural map of filtered monads
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z,\mathbb E_{\infty}}^{\leq*}\!\!\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq*}
(see
[
Rak20
, Construction 4.2.19]
). This produces a map of monads
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z,\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!\!\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
, and, considering corresponding categories of modules, a conservative functor
\DBinAlg\rightarrow\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z)
. We will view the value of this functor as the underlying
\mathbb E_{\infty}
-ring of a derived binomial ring.

Remark 2.61
.

Let us note that the maps of endofunctors
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z,\mathbb E_{\infty}}\to\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}\to\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
all become isomorphisms after applying
-\otimes\mathbb Q
. Indeed, for
A\in\mathrm{Lat}
we have

\mathrm{Sym}(A)\otimes\mathbb Q\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Bin}(A)\otimes\mathbb Q
by
2.28
.
For general
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
, the functor

\mathscr{E}\mathrm{nd}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))\xrightarrow{\circ(-\otimes\mathbb Q)}\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z),{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Q))

commutes with colimits, so it suffices to prove that the maps

\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z,\mathbb E_{\infty}}^{\leq n}\!(M)\otimes\mathbb Q\to\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}Z^{\leq n}(M)\otimes\mathbb Q\to\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(M)\otimes\mathbb Q

are equivalence for each
n
. All three functors are
n
-excisive and so, using Proposition
2.43
, we may assume that
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
. Then, via Proposition
2.33
, we can reduce to the case
M\in\mathrm{Lat}
. In this case the second map is an isomorphism by
2.28
. The first is identified with the natural map
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z,\mathbb E_{\infty}}^{\leq n}\!(M)\simeq M^{\otimes}_{h\Sigma_{n}}\rightarrow H^{0}(M^{\otimes}_{h\Sigma_{n}})\simeq\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}^{n}(M)
which becomes an isomorphism after tensoring with
\mathbb Q
(since
\Sigma_{n}
is a finite group).

Notation 2.62
.

We will denote by
{(-)}^{\circ}\colon\DBinAlg\rightarrow\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z)
the forgetful functor induced by the map of monads
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z,\mathbb E_{\infty}}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
.

The following lemma allows to describe colimits in
\DBinAlg
.

Lemma 2.63
.

The forgetful functor
{(-)}^{\circ}\colon\DBinAlg\rightarrow\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z)
commutes with all small limits and colimits.

Proof.

The argument follows closely the proof of
[
Rak20
, Proposition 4.2.27]
. All small limits and sifted colimits are preserved by
{(-)}^{\circ}
since they can be computed on the level of underlying complexes in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
. Since any small colimit can be realized as a sifted colimit of finite coproducts it remains to check the commutation with a single coproduct.

For
A,B\in\DBinAlg
, let us consider the canonical map
13
13

13

Here
\boxtimes
denotes the coproduct of
\DBinAlg
and
\otimes
denotes the coproduct in
\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z)
.

A^{\circ}\otimes B^{\circ}\overset{\varphi}{\longrightarrow}(A\boxtimes B)^{\circ}
.

To prove that
\varphi
is an equivalence, we will reduce to the case when the binomial rings are free and discrete. First, using that
\DBinAlg
is by definition the category of modules over
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
, we may use Bar-resolutions
A\simeq\colim_{[n]\in\Delta^{{\mathrm{op}}}}\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{(n)}(A)
and
B\simeq\colim_{[n]\in\Delta^{{\mathrm{op}}}}\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{(n)}(B)
to reduce to the case of free derived binomial algebras (here
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{(n)}
denotes the
n
-th iterate of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
). Second, note the
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
is left-adjoint and so commutes with coproducts. Thus, for any
M,N\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
, we can replace
\varphi
with

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(N)^{\circ}\to\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M\oplus N)^{\circ}.

Using that the forgetful functor
\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
is symmetric monoidal (where the monoidal structure on the source is given by the tensor product) and tensor product in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
preserves filtered colimits we can identify the above map with the colimit of a filtered map
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq*}(M)\circledast\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq*}(N)\to\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq*}(M\oplus N)

where
\circledast
is the Day convolution tensor product on
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathbb Z_{\geq 0},{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
. Thus it remains to check that the map

\colim_{i+j\leq n}\left(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq i}(M)\otimes\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq j}(N)\right)\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(M\oplus N)

induced by
\varphi
is an equivalence for any
n
. Considering both sides as sifted colimit preserving polynomial functors
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)\times{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
, using Proposition
2.43
, we can reduce to the case when
M,N\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
, where, using Proposition
2.33
, we can reduce further to the case
M,N\in\mathrm{Lat}
. There it follows from
2.18
(
5
).
∎

Corollary 2.64
.

The forgetful functor
{(-)}^{\circ^{\prime}}\colon\DBinAlg\rightarrow\DAlg(\mathbb Z)
commutes with all small limits and colimits.

Proof.

The forgetful functor
\DAlg(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z)
is conservative and commutes with all small limits and colimits by
[
Rak20
, Proposition 4.2.27]
, and the composition
\DBinAlg\rightarrow\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z)
commutes with all small limits and colimits by
2.63
.
∎

Notation 2.65

(Coproduct in
\DBinAlg
)
.

Note that the forgetful functor
\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\mathscr{D}(\mathbb Z)
is symmetric monoidal, where monoidal structure on the left is given by coproduct. From now on we will denote the coproduct in
\DBinAlg
by
\otimes
for simplicity: by
2.63
and the above this is compatible with the forgetful functor
U\colon\DBinAlg\rightarrow\mathscr{D}(\mathbb Z)
.

Remark 2.66
.

Let
f\colon M\rightarrow N
be a map in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
; it induces a map of derived binomial algebras
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(N)
. Since
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
commutes with colimits one gets an equivalence

\mathbb Z\otimes_{\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)}\!\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(N)\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\cofib(f))

where we identify
\mathbb Z\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(0)
and the map
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)\rightarrow\mathbb Z
is induced by the map
M\rightarrow 0
. In particular, taking
N=0
we get

\mathbb Z\otimes_{\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)}\!\mathbb Z\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M[1]),

in other words
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M[1])
can be computed as the Bar-construction of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)
.

As we saw in
2.53
the computation of the underlying complex of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)
for general
M\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb Z)
is quite involved: in particular, it involves a composition of limit and colimit which a priori don’t commute. It would be more convenient in practice if we knew that not only
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}
, but the colimit
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
also commutes with finite totalizations. However, this is not true in general, see
3.45
. Nevertheless,
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
does commute with finite totalizations of cosimplicial objects if they are suitably bounded from below.

Construction 2.67

(Category
(\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee}
and its ind-completion)
.

Let
(\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee}\subset\Perf(\mathbb Z)
be the image of
\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0,{\mathrm{op}}}\subset\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathrm{op}}
under the duality
\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Perf_{\mathbb Z}
induced by
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{D}(\mathbb Z)}(-,\mathbb Z)
. The category
(\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee}
can be explicitly identified with the subcategory of
\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\geq 0}
spanned by objects
M
with
H^{0}(M)
being torsion-free.

Consider also the ind-completion
\Ind((\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee})
. Under the equivalence
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)\simeq\Ind(\Perf_{\mathbb Z})
the category
\Ind((\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee})
is identified with the full subcategory of
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\geq 0}
spanned by
M
with
H^{0}(M)
being torsion-free.

Remark 2.68
.

Note that for any
n
we have
\mathbf{L}\Gamma^{n}((\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee})\subset(\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee}
. To see this, note that one has

\mathbf{L}\Gamma^{n}(M^{\vee})\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{n}(M)^{\vee},

and
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{n}
preserves the subcategory
\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0}\subset{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
. Indeed, any object in
\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0}
is the geometric realization
|A_{\bullet}|
of an
m
-skeletal simplicial object
A_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Lat})
; by
[
BGMN21
, Proposition 2.10.]
the simplicial object
\mathrm{Sym}^{n}(A_{\bullet})\in\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Lat})
is then
nm
-skeletal, and so its geometric realization is again perfect. Since
\mathbf{L}\Gamma^{n}
preserves filtered colimits this also gives
\mathbf{L}\Gamma^{n}(\Ind((\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee}))\subset\Ind((\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee})
. The same then holds for
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
, since it is a filtered colimit of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}
, that in turn have finite filtrations with the associated graded pieces given by
\oplus_{i=0}^{n}\mathbf{L}\Gamma^{k}
and so preserve
\Ind((\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee})
.

Remark 2.69
.

In particular, we have that for
M\in\Ind((\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee})
, the complexes
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(M)
and
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)
land in the coconnective part
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\geq 0}\subset{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
.

Proposition 2.70
.

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
commutes with finite totalizations of cosimplicial diagrams with values in the subcategory
\Ind((\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee})\subset{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\geq 0}
. In other words, for an
m
-skeletal cosimplicial diagram
M^{\bullet}
in
\Ind((\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee})
the natural map

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\Tot(M^{\bullet}))\rightarrow\Tot(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\bullet}))

in
\DBinAlg
is an equivalence.

Proof.

For a cosimplicial object
X^{\bullet}
let us denote by
\mathrm{Fib}_{n}(X^{\bullet})
the cofiber of the natural map

\Tot(X^{\bullet})\simeq\holim_{[\bullet]\in\Delta}X^{\bullet}\rightarrow\holim_{[\bullet]\in\Delta_{\leq n}}X^{\bullet}.

Fix some
n\geq 0
and let
M^{\bullet}
be an
n
-skeletal cosimplicial diagram with values in
\Ind((\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee})
.
We have a map of fiber sequences

\textstyle{\colim_{m}\mathrm{Fib}_{n}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq m}(M^{\bullet}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\colim_{m}\Tot(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq m}(M^{\bullet}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\colim_{m}\lim_{\Delta_{\leq n}}\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq m}(M^{\bullet})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fib}_{n}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\bullet}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Tot(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\bullet}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\lim_{\Delta_{\leq n}}\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\bullet}).}

(2.5)

Note that by
2.52
the natural map

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq m}(\Tot(M^{\bullet}))\rightarrow\Tot(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq m}(M^{\bullet}))

is an equivalence. So the map in question can be identified with middle vertical arrow in
Equation

2.5
.

Note that the right vertical arrow in
Equation

2.5
is an equivalence since
\Delta_{\leq n}
is final to a finite diagram and filtered colimits commute with finite limits. Note also that all terms in
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq m}(M^{\bullet})
and
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\bullet})
lie in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\geq 0}
and this forces
\mathrm{Fib}_{n}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq m}(M^{\bullet}))
and
\mathrm{Fib}_{n}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\bullet}))
to land in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\geq n}
(see e.g.
[
KP21
, Corollary 3.13]
). It follows that the fiber of the corresponding map
\colim_{m}\mathrm{Fib}_{n}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq m}(M^{\bullet}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Fib}_{n}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\bullet}))
also lies in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\geq n}
and, since the right vertical arrow is an equivalence, the same is true for the fiber of the map

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\Tot(M^{\bullet}))\rightarrow\Tot(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\bullet})).

Since
n
was arbitrary we get that the fiber of the latter is infinitely coconnective, and thus in fact is 0.
∎

Corollary 2.71
.

The restriction of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}\colon{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
to
(\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee}\subset{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
coincides with the right Kan extension of
\mathrm{Bin}\colon\mathrm{Lat}\to{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
along
\mathrm{Lat}\rightarrow(\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee}
.

Proof.

Equivalently, we need to show that the functor
\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0}\rightarrow\DBinAlg^{\mathrm{op}}
sending
M
to
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\vee})
is a left Kan extension of
\mathrm{Bin}((-)^{\vee})\colon\mathrm{Lat}\rightarrow\DBinAlg^{\mathrm{op}}
. Embedding
\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0}
into
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\simeq\mathscr{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathrm{Lat})\subset\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathrm{Lat}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
one sees that to check that a functor
F\colon\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
(where
\mathscr{D}
has small colimits) is a left Kan extension from
\mathrm{Lat}
, it is enough to check that for any
M\in\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0}
and some presentation
M=\colim_{i}M_{i}
as a colimit with
M_{i}\in\mathrm{Lat}
, the natural map
\colim_{i}F(M_{i})\xrightarrow{\sim}F(\colim_{i}M_{i})
is an equivalence. Note that any
M\in\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0}
can be represented as the geometric realization
|M_{i}|
of an
m
-skeletal simplicial diagram
M_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Lat})
. By passing to opposite categories we then reduce to checking that the natural map

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\Tot(M_{\bullet}^{\vee}))\rightarrow\Tot(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M_{\bullet}^{\vee}))

for the dual cosimplicial diagram
M_{\bullet}^{\vee}\in\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\Delta,\mathrm{Lat})
is an equivalence. This is a particular case of
2.70
.
∎

3
Free derived binomial rings

In this section we compute free derived binomial rings on abelian groups concentrated in a single degree, which form the building blocks of the category
\DBinAlg
of derived binomial rings. In the coconnective enough range they turn out to have a natural interpretation as cohomology of Eilenberg-MacLane spaces (see
Section

3.1
); in the connective they rather demonstrate a somewhat singular behavior of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
(see
Section

3.4
). In
Section

3.5
we also compute explicitly what happens in the remaining cases: here
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
no more has a clear topological interpretation, but its cohomology still defines some interesting functors which we fully describe. Besides that, in
Section

3.2
we apply our computations to establish an integral version of homotopy type theory
14
14

14

This was also done earlier in a slightly different context by Horel in
[
Hor22
]
.
; namely, we show that nilpotent spaces of finite type fully faithfully embed in
\DBinAlg
via the singular
\mathbb Z
-cohomology functor (
3.14
). In
Section

3.3
we also give a geometric interpretation of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\vee})
for
M\in\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0}
as cohomology of the structure sheaf of a higher stack
K(\mathbb H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}M)
.

3.1
Cohomology of Eilenberg-MacLane spaces

Most of the coconnective part of the category
\DBinAlg
is in fact glued from the derived binomial rings of the form
C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(X,\mathbb Z)
, as we will see below. Before formulating the precise result, let us first recall some necessary notions.

Construction 3.1

(Generalized Eilenberg-MacLane space)
.

Recall that the category
\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}
of spaces is the sifted completion (or
animation
) of the category
{\mathrm{Set}}^{\mathrm{fin}}
of finite sets. The functor

\mathbb Z[-]\colon{\mathrm{Set}}^{\mathrm{fin}}\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}

sending a finite set
S
to the free abelian group
\mathbb Z[S]
naturally extends to a small colimit preserving functor
\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
, which is known as the singular chains functor
X\mapsto C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)
(and which one could also denote as
X\mapsto\mathbb Z[X]
). It has a right adjoint

K\colon{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}

known as the
generalized Eilenberg-MacLane space
functor.

Recall that
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
can be identified with the category
\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}_{H\mathbb Z}(\mathscr{S}\mathrm{p})
of
H\mathbb Z
-modules in spectra. The forgetful functor
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\mathscr{S}\mathrm{p}
restricts to a functor
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}\mathrm{p}_{\geq 0}
to connective spectra whose left adjoint is given by the smash product
H\mathbb Z\wedge-
. Since
H\mathbb Z\wedge\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}X\simeq C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)

we get that the composition

{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}\mathrm{p}_{\geq 0}\xrightarrow{\Omega^{\infty}}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}

with the infinite loop functor gives another formula for
K
. Since
K
is the right adjoint it commutes will all limits; from the latter formula it also follows that it commutes with sifted colimits by
[
Lur17
, Corollary 4.2.3.5 and Proposition 1.4.3.9]
.

Since both
C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(-,\mathbb Z)
and
K
commute with sifted colimits, given a space
X
which is the geometric realization of a simplicial set
X_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathrm{Set}})
, its singular homology
C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)
can be computed as the geometric realization of the simplicial abelian group
(\mathbb Z[X])_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Ab})
. For
K(-)
, if an object
M
of
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
is the geometric realization
M\simeq|A_{\bullet}|
of
A_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{tf}})
, then
K(M)
is computed by the geometric realization
|A_{\bullet}|\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
where each
A_{i}
considered just as a set.

From the latter description and classical Dold-Kan correspondence (see e.g.
[
Lur17
, Section 1.2.3]
) we see that
\pi_{*}(K(M))\simeq H_{*}(M)
. In particular, for
M\coloneqq A[n]
with
A
a discrete abelian group we get that
\pi_{i}(K(A[n]))=0
if
i\neq n
and
\pi_{n}(K(A[n])=A
, in other words
K(A[n])
is equivalent to the classical
n
-th Eilenberg-MacLane space
K(A,n)
.

Remark 3.2
.

By adjunction, we get an equivalence

\Map_{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}(X,K(M))\simeq\Map_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}}(C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z),M)\simeq K(\tau^{\leq 0}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}(C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z),M)).

For
M=\mathbb Z[n]
this gives
\Map_{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}(X,K(\mathbb Z,n))\simeq K(\tau^{\leq 0}(C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)[n]))
, since for any
X
one has a natural equivalence
(C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z))^{\vee}\simeq C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)
, where
(-)^{\vee}\coloneqq R\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}(-,\mathbb Z)
.

In particular, looking at
\pi_{0}
we get the classical isomorphism
[X,K(\mathbb Z,n)]\simeq H^{n}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)
. This gives a canonical class
[c_{n}]\in H^{n}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(\mathbb Z,n),\mathbb Z)
as an image of
{\mathrm{id}}_{K(\mathbb Z,n)}
, and any class
x\in H^{n}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)
is the pull-back of
[c_{n}]
under the corresponding map
[x]\colon X\rightarrow K(\mathbb Z,n)
.

Construction 3.3
.

By plugging
X=K(M)
in the equivalence

\Map_{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}(X,K(M))\simeq\Map_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}}(C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z),M)

and looking at the image of
{\mathrm{id}}_{K(M)}
we get a natural transformation

C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(K(-),\mathbb Z)\rightarrow{\mathrm{id}}_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}}

(3.1)

of endofunctors of
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
. Both functors commute with sifted colimits and so this transformation is determined by its restriction to
\mathrm{Lat}
(by
2.33
). Note that the corresponding restrictions are given by
M\mapsto M
and
M\mapsto\mathbb Z[M]
, while the transformation is given by the natural homomorphism
\mathbb Z[M]\rightarrow M
that sends a generator
[m]
to
m\in M
.

Post-composing with
(-)^{\vee}
we then get a transformation

(-)^{\vee}\rightarrow C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(K(-),\mathbb Z)

of functors from
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
to
({\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\geq 0})^{\mathrm{op}}
. Since
(-)^{\vee}
sends colimits to limits, the above transformation is also determined by its restriction to
\mathrm{Lat}
, where it is given by the natural embedding

M^{\vee}\rightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{Set}}(M,\mathbb Z)

of
\mathbb Z
-linear functions
M\rightarrow\mathbb Z
into all
\mathbb Z
-valued functions on
M
as a set.

Finally, using the derived binomial ring structure on
C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(K(-),\mathbb Z)
(
2.58
) we can extend this to a transformation

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}((-)^{\vee})\rightarrow C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(K(-),\mathbb Z)

(3.2)

of functors from
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
to
\DBinAlg
.

Example 3.4
.

Let’s evaluate the map (
3.2
) on
\mathbb Z\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
. In this case the map is induced by
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}(\mathbb Z,\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{Set}}(\mathbb Z,\mathbb Z)
, where the source is identified with the constant
\mathbb Z
-valued functions. The corresponding map
\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{Set}}(\mathbb Z,\mathbb Z)
is then identified with the embedding
\Map_{\mathrm{poly}}(\mathbb Z,\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{Set}}(\mathbb Z,\mathbb Z)
. Note that this map is definitely
not
an isomorphism: for example the source is countable, while the target has cardinality of continuum. The transformation (
3.2
) can be thought of as (the derived version of) the embedding of polynomial
\mathbb Z
-valued functions into all.

Even though (
3.2
) is quite far from being an equivalence in the simplest case
M\coloneqq\mathbb Z
, it is in fact one for any perfect
M
that is 1-connective. But, before proving this, let us note the following

Lemma 3.5
.

The functor
C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(K(-),\mathbb Z)\colon\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq-1,{\mathrm{op}}}\rightarrow\DBinAlg
preserves finite coproducts: namely, for any
M_{1},M_{2}\in\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq-1}
the natural map

C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(K(M_{1}),\mathbb Z)\otimes C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(K(M_{2}),\mathbb Z)\rightarrow C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(K(M_{1}\oplus M_{2}),\mathbb Z)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{Lat}[1]
be the image of
\mathrm{Lat}
under the shift functor
[1]\colon{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq-1}
. Note first that the statement is true for
M_{1},M_{2}\in\mathrm{Lat}[1]
. Indeed, if
M\in\mathrm{Lat}[1]
we have
K(M)\simeq S^{1}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}M
, and so it follows from the Künneth formula for singular cohomology.

Note also that the coproduct in
\DBinAlg
commutes with finite totalizations in each variable: indeed, it is enough to check this on the level of underlying objects in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
, where it follows from the fact that finite totalizations are final to a finite limit. Any
M\in\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq-1}
can be realized as the finite geometric realization of a simplicial diagram in
\mathrm{Lat}[1]
; since
C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(K(-),\mathbb Z)
sends geometric realizations to totalizations this way one can reduce from the case of any
M_{1},M_{2}\in\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq-1}
to
M_{1},M_{2}\in\mathrm{Lat}[1]
.
∎

Proposition 3.6
.

For any
M\in\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq-1}
the map (
3.2
)

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\vee})\rightarrow C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(K(M),\mathbb Z)

is an equivalence of derived binomial rings.

Proof.

Note that
\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq-1}
is generated by
\mathbb Z[1]
under finite direct sums and finite geometric realizations. The functor
C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(K(-),\mathbb Z)
sends finite geometric realizations to finite totalizations and finite coproducts to coproducts. By
2.70
this is also true for
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}((-)^{\vee})
restricted to
\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0}
. Thus it is enough to show that (
3.2
) is an equivalence in the case
M=\mathbb Z[1]
.

Note that in this case we have
K(\mathbb Z[1])\simeq K(\mathbb Z,1)\simeq S^{1}
, while
K(0)\simeq\mathrm{pt}
. Evaluating the adjunction
{\mathrm{id}}\rightarrow C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(K(-),\mathbb Z)
on
0\rightarrow\mathbb Z[1]\rightarrow 0
leads to a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbb Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(S^{1},\mathbb Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb Z[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Note that the top horizontal maps split
C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(S^{1},\mathbb Z)
as
\mathbb Z\oplus\mathbb Z[1]
while the vertical middle map is an isomorphism on
H_{1}
(see
) and so is isomorphic to the projection on
\mathbb Z[1]
.

Dually, evaluating
(-)^{\vee}\rightarrow C_{\mathrm{sing}}^{*}(K(-),\mathbb Z)
on
0\rightarrow\mathbb Z[1]\rightarrow 0
we get a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb Z[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(S^{1},\mathbb Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb Z}

in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
with
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(S^{1},\mathbb Z)
being split as
\mathbb Z\oplus\mathbb Z[-1]
, and the middle vertical map being the embedding of the second summand. By adjunction we then get a commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 20.55557pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-20.55557pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb Z}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

with left and right vertical arrows being isomorphisms. Recall that
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z[-1])
has an exhaustive filtration by
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(\mathbb Z[-1])
with the associated graded
\mathbf{L}\Gamma^{*}(\mathbb Z[-1])
. By decalage isomorphism
[
Ill71
, Proposition I.4.3.2.1]
(or
[
KP21
, Proposition A.2.49]
) we have
\mathbf{L}\Gamma^{k}(\mathbb Z[-1])\simeq(\Lambda^{k}\mathbb Z)[-k]
, which is isomorphic to 0 unless
k=0
or
k=1
. This way we get that the underlying complex
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z[-1])
is quasi-isomorphic to
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq 1}(\mathbb Z[-1])
, which canonically splits as
\mathbb Z\oplus\mathbb Z[-1]
by
2.51
(
3
). Moreover, the composite map
\mathbb Z[-1]\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq 1}(\mathbb Z[-1])\rightarrow C^{*}(S^{1},\mathbb Z)
is identified with the initial map
\mathbb Z[-1]\rightarrow C^{*}(S^{1},\mathbb Z)
. Thus the middle vertical arrow induces the identity map
\mathbb Z\oplus\mathbb Z[-1]\rightarrow\mathbb Z\oplus\mathbb Z[-1]
and this ends the proof.

∎

Example 3.7
.

In particular, applying
3.6
to
M
given by a cyclic abelian group in one cohomological degree, we get the following description of free binomial algebras:

•

Plugging
M=\mathbb Z[n]
we get

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z[-n])\simeq C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(\mathbb Z,n),\mathbb Z).

•

For
M=(\mathbb Z/p^{k})[n]
we have
M^{\vee}\simeq\mathbb Z/p^{k}[-n-1]
, and so get an equivalence

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}((\mathbb Z/p^{k})[-n-1])\simeq C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(\mathbb Z/p^{k},n),\mathbb Z).

We would like to emphasize that both equivalences only hold for
n\geq 1
.

Remark 3.8
.

Let
X\in\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}
be a space. Let
x\in H^{n}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)
be a cohomology class; it defines a map
[x]\colon X\rightarrow K(\mathbb Z,n)
and the corresponding map
\mathbb Z[-n]\rightarrow C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)
in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
factors through the universal map
\mathbb Z[-n]\rightarrow C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(\mathbb Z,n),\mathbb Z)
(represented by the canonical class
c_{n}\in H^{n}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(\mathbb Z,n),\mathbb Z)
, see
3.2
): we thus have a commutative triangle

\textstyle{\mathbb Z[-n]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\mapsto c_{n}}
\scriptstyle{1\mapsto x}
\textstyle{C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(\mathbb Z,n),\mathbb Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[x]^{*}}
\textstyle{C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z),}

where the horizontal map is the one from
Equation

3.1
in the case
M=\mathbb Z[n]
. By using the derived binomial ring structure on
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)
we get a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z[-n])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\mapsto c_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{1\mapsto x}
\textstyle{C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(\mathbb Z,n),\mathbb Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[x]^{*}}
\textstyle{C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)}

of derived binomial rings. In other words, the map

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z[-n])\rightarrow C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)

from the free derived binomial ring induced by
x
is described topologically as the pull-back

[x]^{*}\colon C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(\mathbb Z,n),\mathbb Z)\rightarrow C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)

with respect to the map
[x]\colon X\rightarrow K(\mathbb Z,n)
classifying
x\in H_{n}(X,\mathbb Z)
.

3.2
Integral homotopy type theory

In this section we establish a variant of integral homotopy type theory using derived binomial algebras. Our proof is essentially equivalent to the one in
[
Hor22
]
.

The key step is the following lemma

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
N,M\in\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq-1}
. Then the natural map of mapping spaces

\Map_{{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}}(K(N),K(M))\rightarrow\Map_{\DBinAlg}(C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(M),\mathbb Z),C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(N),\mathbb Z))

induced by the functor
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(-,\mathbb Z)\colon{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\DBinAlg
is an equivalence.

Proof.

By
3.6
we can identify

\Map_{\DBinAlg}(C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(M),\mathbb Z),C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(N),\mathbb Z))\xrightarrow{\sim}\Map_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}(M^{\vee},C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(N),\mathbb Z))

(3.3)

with the map given by composing the underlying map of complexes with the map
M^{\vee}\rightarrow C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(M),\mathbb Z)
from
3.3
. On the other hand, by
C_{*}(-,\mathbb Z)\dashv K
adjunction we get an equivalence

\Map_{{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}}(K(N),K(M))\simeq\Map_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}(C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(K(N),\mathbb Z),M)\simeq\Map_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}(M^{\vee},C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(N),\mathbb Z))

with the map from the left to the right again given by composing the induced map
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(M),\mathbb Z)\rightarrow C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(N),\mathbb Z)
with
M^{\vee}\rightarrow C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(M),\mathbb Z)
. This way we get that the map in question composed with equivalence
Equation

3.3
is an equivalence. Thus so is the original map.
∎

Notation 3.10
.

For a derived binomial ring
B\in\DBinAlg
let us denote

[B]\coloneqq\Map_{\DBinAlg}(B,\mathbb Z)\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}.

We will call
[B]
the
homotopy type
defined by
B
. Functor
[-]
sends colimits of derived binomial algebras to limits of spaces.

Remark 3.11
.

Note that for any space
X\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
, the natural identification
X\simeq\Map_{{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}}(\{*\},X)
induces a comparison map

c_{X}\colon X\rightarrow\Map_{\DBinAlg}(C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z),\mathbb Z)=:[C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)].

Proposition 3.12

(
[
Hor22
, Theorem 5.4]
)
.

Let
X\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
be connected nilpotent space of finite type. Then the natural map

c_{X}\colon X\rightarrow[C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)]

is an equivalence.

Proof.

By
[
GJ09
, Proposition V.6.1]
any
X
connected nilpotent of finite type can be expressed as the homotopy limit of a tower

\ldots\rightarrow X_{i}\rightarrow X_{i-1}\rightarrow\ldots\rightarrow X_{1}\rightarrow X_{0}=*

with the property that each map
X_{i}\rightarrow X_{i-1}
is a fiber of a map
X_{i-1}\rightarrow K(A_{i},k_{i})
with
k_{i}\geq 2
and
k_{i}\rightarrow\infty
as
i\rightarrow\infty
, and
A_{i}
being a finitely generated abelian group for any
i
. By
3.9
applied to
N=0
one sees that
c_{X}
is an equivalence for
X\simeq K(M)
and
M\in\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq-1}
, in particular it is true for
X_{0}\simeq K(0)
and
K(A_{i},k_{i})\simeq K(A_{i}[k_{i}])
above. Let us show by induction on
i
that it is also true for
X_{i}
. Indeed, by Eilenberg-Moore theorem (see e.g.
[
Toë20
, Theorem 5.4]
) and
2.63
the natural map

C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X_{i-1},\mathbb Z)\otimes_{C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(A_{i},k_{i}),\mathbb Z)}\mathbb Z\rightarrow C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X_{i},\mathbb Z)

induced by the fiber square

\textstyle{X_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{*\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(A_{i},k_{i})}

is an equivalence, which allows to identify

[C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X_{i},\mathbb Z)]\simeq[C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X_{i-1},\mathbb Z)]\times_{[C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(A_{i},k_{i}),\mathbb Z)]}[\mathbb Z],

and then
X_{i}
with
[C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X_{i},\mathbb Z)]
.

Finally, since
k_{i}\rightarrow\infty
as
i
grows we know that

C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)\simeq\colim_{i}C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X_{i},\mathbb Z)\in\DBinAlg,

and so

[C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)]\simeq\lim_{i}[C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X_{i},\mathbb Z)].

By functoriality we get that
c_{X}=\lim_{i}c_{X_{i}}
is an equivalence.
∎

Remark 3.13
.

Following the same line of argumentation as in
[
Hor22
, Section 6]
, more generally for
X
connected of finite type one can identify
c_{X}\colon X\rightarrow[C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)]
with the
H\mathbb Z
-localization map
X\rightarrow L_{\mathbb Z}X
.

Corollary 3.14
.

The restriction of the functor
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(-,\mathbb Z)\colon{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\DBinAlg
to the full subcategory spanned by connected nilpotent spaces of finite type is fully faithful.

Proof.

It is enough to show that the natural map

\Map_{{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}}(X,Y)\rightarrow\Map_{\DBinAlg}(C^{*}_{{\mathrm{sing}}}(Y,\mathbb Z),C^{*}_{{\mathrm{sing}}}(X,\mathbb Z))

is an equivalence if
Y
is connected nilpotent of finite type. Note that since
X=\colim_{X}\{*\}\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
we can rewrite the above map as

\lim_{X}\left(\Map_{{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}}(\{*\},Y)\rightarrow\Map_{\DBinAlg}(C^{*}_{{\mathrm{sing}}}(Y,\mathbb Z),\mathbb Z)\right)\simeq\lim_{X}\left(Y\rightarrow[C^{*}_{{\mathrm{sing}}}(Y,\mathbb Z)]\right).

It remains to refer to
3.12
to see that the latter map is an equivalence.
∎

Remark 3.15
.

In
[
Eke02
, Theorem 2.9]
Ekedahl shows that any nilpotent space
X
is equivalent to a geometric realization
|X_{\bullet}|
of a simplicial set
X_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathrm{Set}})
where
X_{0}\coloneqq*
is a point, and all terms
X_{i}
can be endowed with the structures of finite free abelian groups so that all the maps
X_{i}\rightarrow X_{j}
induced by the simplicial structure on
X_{\bullet}
are polynomial in the sense of
2.1
(Ekedahl uses the term
“numerical”
instead of polynomial). Note that a polynomial map
X_{i}\rightarrow X_{j}
induces a
\mathbb Z
-linear map
\mathbb Z[X_{i}]\rightarrow X_{j}
via
2.5
, and then, dually, a linear map
X_{j}^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathrm{Bin}(X_{i}^{\vee})
, which in turn defines a map
\mathrm{Bin}(X_{j}^{\vee})\rightarrow\mathrm{Bin}(X_{i}^{\vee})
of classical binomial algebras. As a part of
[
Eke02
, Theorem 2.9]
he also shows that
X_{\bullet}
can be chosen so that
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)
is computed by the corresponding totalization
\Tot_{[\bullet]\in\Delta}\mathrm{Bin}(X_{\bullet}^{\vee})
. In our terminology, one gets an equivalence of derived binomial rings

C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)\xrightarrow{\sim}\Tot_{[\bullet]\in\Delta}\mathrm{Bin}(X_{\bullet}^{\vee}).

In particular, one can choose
X_{\bullet}
to be
“special and minimal”
in the terminology of
loc.cit.
, which, as Ekedahl shows, enjoys some uniqueness and functoriality properties on the (homotopy-)simplicial level (as a functor in
X
).

3.3
Interpretation as cohomology of generalized classifying stacks

Let
R
be a classical commutative ring.

Construction 3.16

(Higher stacks)
.

Let
\Aff_{\!/\!R}
be the category of (classical) affine schemes over
R
. We endow
\Aff_{\!/\!R}
with the Grothendieck topology generated by faithfully flat covers. We let
\Stk_{\!/\!R}
denote the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{h}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}^{{\mathrm{fpqc}}}(\Aff_{\!/\!R},\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc})\subset\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\Aff_{\!/\!R}^{{\mathrm{op}}},{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
of
higher stacks
: namely, the category of
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
-valued hypersheaves in fpqc topology on
\Aff_{\!/\!R}
.

Having a higher stack
\mathpzc Y
with a fixed point
y\in\mathpzc Y(\Spec R)
(which by Yoneda lemma can also be viewed as the map
y\colon\{*\}\rightarrow\mathpzc Y
, where
\{*\}=\Spec R
is the final object of
\Stk_{\!/\!R}
) one can consider the corresponding
homotopy group sheaves

\{\pi_{i}(\mathpzc Y,y)\}_{i\in\mathbb N}\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}^{{\mathrm{fpqc}}}(\Aff_{\!/\!R},{\mathrm{Set}})

defined as sheafification of the presheaves
X\mapsto\pi_{i}(\mathpzc Y(X),y)\in{\mathrm{Set}}
. As usual, for
i\geq 1
the sheaves
\pi_{i}(\mathpzc Y,y)
have a natural structure of sheaves of groups, that are in fact commutative if
i\geq 2
.

One can extend a lot of cohomology theories to higher stacks; here we will mostly be interested only in the following:

Construction 3.17

(
\mathscr{O}
-cohomology)
.

Consider the functor
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}^{\mathrm{cl}}\colon\Aff^{{\mathrm{op}}}_{\!/\!R}\rightarrow\DAlg(R)
sending an affine scheme
\Spec A
to
A
considered as a derived commutative
R
-algebra; note that by the classical faithfully flat descent this is an fpqc-sheaf. Taking right Kan extension along the embedding
\Aff_{\!/\!R}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\Stk_{\!/\!R}^{\mathrm{op}}
we obtain a functor

\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}\colon\Stk_{\!/\!R}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\DAlg(R).

Since
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}^{\mathrm{cl}}
is a sheaf, one can see that
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}\colon\Stk_{\!/\!R}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(R)
sends colimits in
\Stk_{\!/\!R}
to limits in
\DAlg(R)
.

It will also be useful to have a suitable notion of abelian group (higher) stack. The idea of the following definition goes back to Lawyere.

Construction 3.18

(Abelian group objects)
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-category admitting finite products. An
abelian group object
of
\mathscr{C}
by definition is a functor
A\colon\mathrm{Lat}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
that commutes with finite products. We will denote by
\mathrm{Ab}(\mathscr{C})\subset\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathrm{Lat}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{C})
the full subcategory spanned by abelian group objects of
\mathscr{C}
. One has the forgetful functor
K\colon\mathrm{Ab}(\mathscr{C})\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
given by evaluation at the object
\mathbb Z\in\mathrm{Lat}^{\mathrm{op}}
. Functor
K
commutes with limits and, since sifted colimits commute with products, also with sifted colimits.

In the case
\mathscr{C}={\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
, the category
\mathrm{Ab}({\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
is naturally identified with
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
(
[
Lur17a
, Example 1.2.9]
). The functor
K\colon\mathrm{Ab}({\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
is then naturally identified with the generalized Eilenberg-MacLane space functor that we considered in
3.1
; recall that it has a left adjoint
\mathbb Z[-]\colon{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ab}({\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
given by the
“free abelian group”
. More generally, for
\mathscr{C}=\Stk_{\!/\!R}
the functor
K\colon\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{\!/\!R})\rightarrow\Stk_{\!/\!R}
also has a left adjoint given by applying
\mathbb Z[-]
on the level of presheaves, and then sheafification.

We will consider the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{\!/\!R})
of
abelian group
R
-stacks
. By
[
Lur17a
, Example 1.2.13]

\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{\!/\!R})
is naturally identified with the tensor product
\Stk_{\!/\!R}\otimes{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
, which, in turn, can be further identified with the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{h}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}^{{\mathrm{fpqc}}}(\Aff_{\!/\!R},{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})
of
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
-valued hypersheaves on
\Aff_{\!/\!R}
. One has a natural embedding
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}^{{\mathrm{fpqc}}}(\Aff_{\!/\!R},\mathrm{Ab})\subset\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{\!/\!R})
; in particular, any classical abelian group
R
-scheme naturally defines an object of
\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{\!/\!R})
.

Remark 3.19
.

Note that given an abelian group
R
-stack
H\colon\mathrm{Lat}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\Stk_{\!/\!R}
, evaluating
H
on the unique map
0\rightarrow\mathbb Z
in
\mathrm{Lat}^{\mathrm{op}}
endows
K(H)\coloneqq H(\mathbb Z)
with canonical point
0\colon\Spec R=H(0)\rightarrow H(\mathbb Z)\simeq K(H)
. We then can consider the corresponding homotopy group sheaves
\pi_{i}(K(H),0)
as in
3.16
, which we will simply denote
\pi_{i}(H)
. Under the identification
\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{\!/\!R})\simeq\mathrm{h}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}^{{\mathrm{fpqc}}}(\Aff_{\!/\!R},{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})
, the homotopy groups
\pi_{i}(H)
correspond to the classical sheaves
\mathcal{H}^{-i}(H)\in\mathrm{h}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}^{{\mathrm{fpqc}}}(\Aff_{\!/\!R},\mathrm{Ab})
given by
(-i)
-th cohomology. E.g. by
[
BS13
, Proposition 3.3.3]
, Postnikov towers converge in
\mathrm{h}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}^{{\mathrm{fpqc}}}(\Aff_{\!/\!R},{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})
; consequently we have Whitehead theorem in this setting: a map
H_{1}\rightarrow H_{2}
in
\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{\!/\!R})
is an equivalence if and only if it induces an isomorphism on all
\pi_{i}
.

Remark 3.20
.

Given an abelian group stack
H\in\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{\!/\!R})
, one has a natural colimit preserving functor
H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}-\colon{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{\!/\!R})
given by tensor product with
H
:
M\mapsto H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}M\in\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{\!/\!R})
. One can view it via
2.33
as the unique sifted colimit preserving functor that sends a finitely generated free abelian group
A\in\mathrm{Lat}
to
H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}A
. Since
K\colon\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{\!/\!R})\rightarrow\Stk_{\!/\!R}
commutes with sifted colimits, and
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}
sends colimits in stacks to limits in
\DAlg(R)
the functor

\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(K(H\otimes_{{\mathbb Z}}-))\colon{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0,{\mathrm{op}}}\rightarrow\DAlg(R)

sends sifted colimits in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
to limits in
\DAlg(R)
.

In the case
M=\mathbb Z[n]
the tensor product
H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Z[n]\simeq H[n]
is often denoted as
B^{n}H
. Also, in the case
M=\mathbb Z/n
we will denote
H\otimes_{{\mathbb Z}}\mathbb Z/n
by
[H/n]
(meaning
“the quotient stack via the action through multiplication by
n
”
). Indeed, since
\mathbb Z/n\simeq\cofib(\mathbb Z\xrightarrow{\cdot n}\mathbb Z)
we have
[H/n]\simeq\cofib(H\xrightarrow{\cdot n}H)\in\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{\!/\!R})
.

Construction 3.21

(The map
i\colon\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathbb H
)
.

Let
R=\mathbb Z
and let
\mathbb H=\Spec(\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z))
. Let
\underline{\mathbb Z}
be the hypersheafification of the constant presheaf given by
\mathbb Z
.
One has
\underline{\mathbb Z}=\mathbb Z[*]
, and so by adjunction, abelian group sheaves homomorphisms
\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathbb H
in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}^{{\mathrm{fpqc}}}(\Aff_{\!/\!R},\mathrm{Ab})
are in bijection with
\mathbb H(\Spec\mathbb Z)
or, in other words, algebra homomorphisms
\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\mathbb Z
. There is a preferred such homomorphism
\ev_{1}\colon\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\mathbb Z
given by the binomial ring structure on
\mathbb Z
(
2.32
(
1
)), and we will denote the corresponding map of group sheaves
i\colon\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathbb H
. On the level of functions (after applying
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}
) the pull-back
i^{*}
is given by the natural embedding
\Map_{\mathrm{poly}}(\mathbb Z,\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\Map_{{\mathrm{Set}}}(\mathbb Z,\mathbb Z)
of polynomial functions into all.

Remark 3.22

(
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\vee})\in\DAlg(\mathbb Z)
as
\mathscr{O}
-cohomology)
.

Note that via the discussion in
2.5
and definition of
\mathrm{Bin}
we have a natural in
A\in\mathrm{Lat}
identification

\Spec(\mathrm{Bin}(A^{\vee}))\simeq\Spec(\Map_{\mathrm{poly}}(A,\mathbb Z))\simeq\mathbb H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}A

as group
\mathbb Z
-schemes. By taking back the global sections of
\mathscr{O}
, we get the identification of the restriction of the functor

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}((-)^{\vee})\colon\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0,{\mathrm{op}}}\rightarrow\DAlg(\mathbb Z)

to
\mathrm{Lat}^{{\mathrm{op}}}\subset\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0,{\mathrm{op}}}
with
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(K(\mathbb H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}-))
. Since both functors send finite geometric realizations to totalizations one gets from this an identification of functors

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}((-)^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(K(\mathbb H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}-));

in particular, for any
M\in\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0}
we get a natural equivalence

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(K(\mathbb H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}M))

of derived commutative
\mathbb Z
-algebras.

Remark 3.23
.

Similarly, having
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
we can consider the associated constant abelian group stack
\underline{M}\in\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{/\mathbb Z})
and its underlying stack
K(\underline{M})
. Then we claim that there is a natural equivalence

C^{*}_{{\mathrm{sing}}}(K(-),\mathbb Z)\simeq\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(K(-))

of functors
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0,{\mathrm{op}}}\rightarrow\DAlg(\mathbb Z)
. There are various ways to see this; for example, both functors send sifted colimits in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
to limits in
\DAlg(\mathbb Z)
, and their restrictions to
\mathrm{Lat}^{\mathrm{op}}\subset{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0,{\mathrm{op}}}
can both be identified with the functor

\Map_{{\mathrm{Set}}}(-,\mathbb Z)\colon\mathrm{Lat}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\DAlg(\mathbb Z)

sending a lattice
A\in\mathrm{Lat}
to the algebra of all
\mathbb Z
-valued functions on
A
.

Remark 3.24
.

The map
i\colon\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathbb H
that we constructed in
3.21
induces a natural in
M
map
M\rightarrow\mathbb H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}M
of abelian group stacks. By applying
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}
we then get a transformation

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}((-)^{\vee})\simeq\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(K(\mathbb H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}-)){\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(K(-))\simeq C^{*}_{{\mathrm{sing}}}(K(-),\mathbb Z)

of functors
\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0,{\mathrm{op}}}\rightarrow\DAlg(\mathbb Z)
.
Moreover, its restriction to
\mathrm{Lat}^{{\mathrm{op}}}
is identified with the transformation
\Map_{\mathrm{poly}}(-,\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{Set}}(-,\mathbb Z)
, and (since both functors sends finite geometric realizations to totalizations) this way it coincides with the transformation
Equation

3.2
(or rather its composition with the forgetful functor
\DBinAlg\rightarrow\DAlg(\mathbb Z)
). In particular, from
3.6
we then get the following:

Corollary 3.25

(
[
Toë20
, Corollary 3.4]
)
.

Let
M\in\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq-1,{\mathrm{op}}}
. Then the map
\underline{M}\rightarrow\mathbb H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}M
provided by tensoring
i\colon\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathbb H
with
M
induces an equivalence of derived commutative algebras

\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(K(\mathbb H\otimes_{\mathbb Z}M))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(K(\underline{M})).

3.4
Free derived binomial rings in other degrees

In this section we finish the computation of free binomial algebras in the remaining cohomological degrees. We will first deal with the connective part, where the description is surprisingly easy, and then describe what happens in the remaining cases given by torsion abelian groups put in cohomological degree 1.

Let us start with the following lemma:

Lemma 3.26
.

For any
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
the map of derived commutative rings

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(M\otimes\mathbb Q)\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M\otimes\mathbb Q)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The above map is filtered with respect to exhaustive filtrations, so it is enough to show an analogous statement for
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq n}(M\otimes\mathbb Q)\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(M\otimes\mathbb Q)
. Both sides are
n
-excisive functors in
M
that commute with sifted colimits, so it is enough to check that the map is an equivalence for
M\in\mathrm{Lat}
. This follows from
3.26
.
∎

Using the identification in
3.26
, for any
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
,
M\rightarrow M\otimes\mathbb Q
induces a natural map

\alpha\colon\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(M\otimes\mathbb Q).

Quite surprisingly, it turns out to be an equivalence for any
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq-1}
:

Proposition 3.27
.

Let
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq-1}
. Then the map
\alpha
induces an equivalence of derived commutative algebras

\alpha\colon\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(M\otimes\mathbb Q).

Proof.

Both
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
(or rather
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\circ^{\prime}}\!\colon{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\DAlg(\mathbb Z)
in the notations of
2.64
) and
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}\colon{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\DAlg(\mathbb Z)
commute with all colimits. Since
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq-1}
is generated under colimits by
M=\mathbb Z[1]
it would be enough to check the statement in this case.

Note that the functor
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Q)\times\prod_{p}{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb F_{p})
given by

N\mapsto N_{\mathbb Q}\times\prod_{p}(N\otimes\mathbb F_{p})

is conservative. Thus to check that
\alpha
induces an equivalence it is enough to check this after tensoring with
\mathbb Q
and modulo each prime
p
. For tensor with
\mathbb Q
we have
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)\otimes\mathbb Q\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(M)\otimes\mathbb Q
for any
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
by the end of
2.61
, and
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(M)\otimes\mathbb Q\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(M\otimes\mathbb Q)\otimes\mathbb Q
(both functors are direct sums of
n
-excisive sifted colimit commuting and agree on
M\in\mathrm{Lat}
). Modulo
p
we have the following:

Lemma 3.28
.

The natural map

\mathbb F_{p}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z[1])\otimes\mathbb F_{p}
(from the initial derived commutative ring over
\mathbb F_{p}
) is an equivalence.

Proof.

Note that by
2.66
one can compute
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z[1])
as
\mathbb Z\otimes_{\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)}^{\mathbb L}\mathbb Z
. For the reduction mod
p
we have

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z[1])\otimes\mathbb F_{p}\simeq\mathbb F_{p}\otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)/p}\mathbb F_{p}.

Note that by
2.15
the algebra
\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)/p
is identified with
\colim_{n}\Map_{\mathrm{Set}}(\mathbb Z/p^{n},\mathbb F_{p})
with maps induced by reductions
\ldots\rightarrow\mathbb Z/p^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb Z/p^{n-1}\rightarrow\ldots\rightarrow\mathbb Z/p
. The restriction of homomorphism
\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)/p\rightarrow\mathbb F_{p}
to
\Map(\mathbb Z/p^{n},\mathbb F_{p})\subset\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)/p
is given by the pull-back under the embedding
0\hookrightarrow\mathbb Z/p^{n}
. Note that the latter map is an open embedding (if we consider the above groups as constant group schemes over
\mathbb F_{p}
) and thus
\mathbb F_{p}\simeq\Map_{\mathrm{Set}}(0,\mathbb F_{p})
is a flat
\Map_{\mathrm{Set}}(\mathbb Z/p^{n},\mathbb F_{p})
-module. By passing to colimit over
n
it follows that
\mathbb F_{p}
is a flat
\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)/p
-module. Thus we get

\mathbb F_{p}\otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)/p}\mathbb F_{p}\simeq\mathbb F_{p}\otimes_{\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)/p}\mathbb F_{p}\simeq\mathbb F_{p},

as desired.
∎

It is also true that the map
\mathbb F_{p}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(\mathbb Z[1]\otimes\mathbb Q)\otimes\mathbb F_{p}
is an equivalence: indeed,
\mathbb F_{p}
maps isomorphically to
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}^{0}\otimes\mathbb F_{p}
, while
