\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}^{i}(\mathbb Z[1]\otimes\mathbb Q)
is a
\mathbb Q
-module for
i>0
, and so

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}^{i}(\mathbb Z[1]\otimes\mathbb Q)\otimes\mathbb F_{p}\simeq 0
. Thus, from this and the lemma above we get that the map

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z[1])\otimes\mathbb F_{p}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(\mathbb Z[1])\otimes\mathbb F_{p}

is an equivalence for every
p
, and so
\alpha\colon\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z[1])\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb Z}(\mathbb Q[1])
is also an equivalence.

∎

Remark 3.29
.

In particular, we get that if
A
is a torsion abelian group (meaning
A\otimes\mathbb Q\simeq 0
) one has
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A[n])=\mathbb Z
for
n\geq 1
.

In fact, the statement in
3.29
can be extended slightly to the right in the cohomological direction:

Proposition 3.30
.

Let
A\in\mathrm{Ab}
be a torsion abelian group (meaning
A\otimes\mathbb Q\simeq 0
). Then the natural map

\mathbb Z\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The proof is somewhat similar to
3.27
. It is enough to check that the map is an equivalence after tensoring with
\mathbb Q
and modulo each prime
p
. It is enough to consider the case
A=\mathbb Z/p^{k}
. We have
\mathbb Z/p^{k}=\cofib(\mathbb Z\xrightarrow{\cdot p^{k}}\mathbb Z)
and so

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z/p^{k})\simeq\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\otimes_{\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)}^{\mathbb L}\mathbb Z

where the map
\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\rightarrow\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)
is induced by multiplication by
p^{k}
on
\mathbb Z
. In terms of the group scheme
\mathbb H\coloneqq\Spec(\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z))
the latter map is the pull-back with respect to the multiplication by
p^{k}
map
[p^{k}]\colon\mathbb H\xrightarrow{}\mathbb H
. Over
\mathbb Q
we have
\mathbb H_{\mathbb Q}\simeq\mathbb G_{a,\mathbb Q}
and multiplication by
p^{k}
is an isomorphism, so

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z/p^{k})\otimes\mathbb Q\simeq\mathbb Q.

Modulo
\ell\neq p
we have
\mathbb H_{\mathbb F_{\ell}}=\Spec(\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\otimes\mathbb F_{\ell})\simeq\lim_{n}\underline{\mathbb Z/\ell^{n}}
and multiplication by
p^{k}
is again an isomorphism. So,

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z/p^{k})\otimes\mathbb F_{\ell}\simeq\mathbb F_{\ell}.

It remains to understand the case
\ell=p
. We have
\mathbb H_{\mathbb F_{p}}=\Spec(\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\otimes\mathbb F_{p})\simeq\lim_{n}\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}
. Multiplication by
p^{k}
is no longer an isomorphism, but it is an open embedding: indeed, via the above identification we have a fiber square

\textstyle{\mathbb H_{\mathbb F_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cdot p^{k}}
\textstyle{\mathbb H_{\mathbb F_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{k}},}

so the map
\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\otimes\mathbb F_{p}\rightarrow\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\otimes\mathbb F_{p}
is the pushout of the surjection
\Map_{\mathrm{Set}}(\mathbb Z/p^{k},\mathbb F_{p})\rightarrow\mathbb F_{p}
(induced by the open embedding
\{0\}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{k}}
of constant
\mathbb F_{p}
-group schemes) with respect to the map
\Map_{\mathrm{Set}}(\mathbb Z/p^{k},\mathbb F_{p})\rightarrow\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\otimes\mathbb F_{p}
. In particular, it is flat and surjective at the same moment. From this and
2.66
we get

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z/p^{k})\otimes\mathbb F_{p}\simeq(\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\otimes\mathbb F_{p})\otimes_{(\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\otimes\mathbb F_{p})}\mathbb F_{p}\simeq\mathbb F_{p},

and then also the equivalence
\mathbb Z\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z/p^{k})
.
∎

Remark 3.31
.

For any abelian group
A
we have a short exact sequence
0\rightarrow\mathrm{Tors}(A)\rightarrow A\rightarrow A^{\mathrm{tf}}\rightarrow 0
where
\mathrm{Tors}(A)
is the maximal torsion subgroup and
A^{\mathrm{tf}}
is torsion free. On the level of derived categories we have
A^{\mathrm{tf}}\simeq\cofib(\mathrm{Tors}(A)\rightarrow A)
, so by
2.66
we get

\mathrm{Bin}(A^{\mathrm{tf}})\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A^{\mathrm{tf}})\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A)\otimes_{\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathrm{Tors}(A))}\mathbb Z.

However, by
3.30

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathrm{Tors}(A))\simeq\mathbb Z
. This shows that there is a natural equivalence

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A)\simeq\mathrm{Bin}(A^{\mathrm{tf}})

for any abelian group
A
, in particular
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A)
only depends on
A^{\mathrm{tf}}
.

3.5
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])
for torsion
A

It remains to understand the structure of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])
where
A
is a torsion abelian group. Let
\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{p}\subset\mathrm{Ab}
denote the subcategory of finite abelian groups of
p
-power order. Using that any torsion abelian group is a filtered colimit of finite abelian groups, and that
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
sends direct sums to (derived) tensor products, we can reduce to the case
A\in\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{p}
for some prime
p
.

Recall the following description of
\mathbb H
modulo
p^{d}
, generalizing one in
2.15
:

Lemma 3.32

(
[
Toë20
, Corollaire 2.3]
)
.

For any
n\geq 1
there is a natural isomorphism of affine group schemes over
\mathbb Z/p^{n}

\mathbb H_{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{\mathbb Z_{p}}\coloneqq\lim_{i\geq 0}\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{i}}

such that the composition with the map
i\colon\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathbb H
is the natural map
\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\underline{\mathbb Z_{p}}
.

Proof.

Note that

(t-1)^{p^{n-1+s}}\equiv(t^{p^{s}}-1)^{p^{n-1}}\pmod{p^{n}}.

(3.4)

We see that for any
s
, the map
\mu_{p^{s},\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\ \rightarrow\mathbb G_{m,\mathbb Z/p^{n}}
(on the level of functions given by the projection
\mathbb Z/p^{n}[t,t^{-1}]\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb Z/p^{n}[t,t^{-1}]/(t^{p^{s}}-1)
) factors through the formal completion
\widehat{\mathbb G}_{m,\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\subset\mathbb G_{m,\mathbb Z/p^{n}}
. For any
s\geq 1
we get maps
\mu_{p^{s},\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbb G}_{m,\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\rightarrow\mathbb G_{m,\mathbb Z/p^{n}}
and, passing to Cartier duals, also maps
\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathbb H_{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{s}}
with the composition given by the natural projection.

Moreover, we claim that the induced map
\colim_{s\geq 1}(\mu_{p^{s},\mathbb Z/p^{n}})\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbb G}_{m,\mathbb Z/p^{n}}
is an isomorphism. Indeed, we need to check that the induced map

\lim_{s}\mathbb Z/p^{n}[t,t^{-1}]/(t-1)^{p^{s}}\rightarrow\lim_{s}\mathbb Z/p^{n}[t,t^{-1}]/(t^{p^{s}}-1)

is an isomorphism.
This follows from the
Equation

3.4
and the embedding of ideals
(t^{p^{n+s-1}}-1)\subset(t-1)^{p^{s}}\pmod{p^{n}}
, which one can show by decomposing
t
as
(t-1)+1
and keeping track of
p
-adic valuations of the binomial coefficients appearing.
∎

Notation 3.33
.

For
A\in\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{p}
, let us denote by
A^{D}\coloneqq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}(A,\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)
the Pontryagin dual of
A
. Using the short exact sequence
0\rightarrow\mathbb Z\rightarrow\mathbb Q\rightarrow\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z\rightarrow 0
one also sees that
A^{D}\simeq\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{Ab}}(A,\mathbb Z)\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}(A,\mathbb Z)[1]\simeq A^{\vee}[1]
.

For
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
let us also denote by
\mathbf{L}^{i}\mathrm{Bin}(M)\in\mathrm{Ab}
the
i
-th cohomology of
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)
:

\mathbf{L}^{i}\mathrm{Bin}(M)\coloneqq H^{i}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M)),

and similarly for other non-abelian derived functors.

Remark 3.34
.

For any
k\in\mathbb Z
multiplication by
k
on
A
induces a map on
\mathbf{L}\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{n}(A[-1])
which is multiplication by
k^{n}
. In particular, if
A
is finite, multiplication by
|A|
on
A
induces a zero map on
\mathbf{L}\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{n}(A[-1])
; consequently,
\mathbf{L}^{i}\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{n}(A[-1])
is a finite abelian group killed by
|A|^{n}
for any
i
.

This way, if
A\in\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{p}
, we get that
\mathbf{L}^{i}\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{n}(A[-1])\in\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{p}
for any
n>0
and
i\in\mathbb Z
. More generally, since
\mathbf{L}^{i}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(A[-1])
has a finite filtration with associated graded pieces given by
\mathbf{L}^{i}\Gamma_{\mathbb Z}^{n}(A[-1])
we get that
\mathbf{L}^{i}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(A[-1])
is also in
\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{p}
if
i\neq 0
. Also, by
2.51
(
2
),
\mathbf{L}^{0}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(A[-1])
splits as

\mathbf{L}^{0}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(A[-1])\simeq\mathbb Z\oplus\mathbf{L}^{0}\overline{\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}}(A[-1]),

where the second summand is in
\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{p}
.

Lemma 3.35
.

Let
A\in\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{p}
and fix
n\geq 1
. Then

1.

The natural map

i\colon\underline{A}\rightarrow\mathbb H_{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\otimes A

in
\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{/\mathbb Z/p^{n}})
given by tensoring
i\colon\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathbb H_{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}
with
A
is an equivalence.

2.

There are natural equivalences

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])\otimes_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathbb L}\mathbb Z/p^{n}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(K(\mathbb H_{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\otimes A^{D}))\simeq\Map_{\mathrm{Set}}(A^{D},\mathbb Z/p^{n})

of derived commutative rings.

3.

\mathbf{L}^{i}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])=0
unless
i=0
or 1;
\mathbf{L}^{0}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])=\mathbb Z
and
\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])
is torsion and
p
-divisible.

Proof.

Part 1 follows from the repleteness of fpqc-topos
[
Toë20
]
. Namely, we know that
\mathbb H_{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\simeq\lim_{i}\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{i}}
as affine group schemes. By
[
Toë20
, Proposition B.1]
homotopy group sheaves (see
3.16
) commute with sequential colimits and so it is also true that
\mathbb H_{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\in\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{/{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}})
computes
\holim_{i}\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{i}}\in\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{/{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}})
. Since
A
is finite it lies in
\Perf_{\mathbb Z}
and so we can move
-\otimes_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathbb L}A
inside the limit

\mathbb H_{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathbb L}A\simeq\holim_{i}(\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{i}}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathbb L}A)\in\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{/\mathbb Z/p^{n}}).

In the last limit we can restrict to
i\gg 0
, where both
\pi_{0}(\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{i}}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathbb L}A)\simeq A/p^{i}
and
\pi_{1}(\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{i}}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathbb L}A)=A[p^{i}]
15
15

15

Here, abusing notation, we denote by
[p^{i}]
the elementary
p^{i}
-torsion, and not homological shift by
p^{i}
.
will be given by
A
. The maps in the diagram are identity on
\pi_{0}
and multiplication by
p
on
\pi_{1}
, so we get

\pi_{0}(\holim_{i}(\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{i}}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathbb L}A))\simeq\holim(\ldots\xrightarrow{{\mathrm{id}}}\underline{A}\xrightarrow{{\mathrm{id}}}\underline{A}\xrightarrow{{\mathrm{id}}}\underline{A})\simeq\underline{A}

and

\pi_{1}(\holim_{i}(\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{i}}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathbb L}A))\simeq\holim(\ldots\xrightarrow{\cdot p}\underline{A}\xrightarrow{\cdot p}\underline{A}\xrightarrow{\cdot p}\underline{A})\simeq 0

(and all
\pi_{j}
for
j>1
are equal to 0). Thus we get that the natural map

\mathbb H_{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathbb L}A\rightarrow\pi_{0}(\mathbb H_{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathbb L}A)\simeq\underline{A}

is an equivalence. The map

i\colon\underline{A}\rightarrow\mathbb H_{\mathbb Z/p^{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathbb L}A
is given by
\underline{A}\simeq\underline{\mathbb Z}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}A\rightarrow\holim_{i\gg 0}(\underline{\mathbb Z/p^{i}}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}^{\mathbb L}A)
which maps identically to
\pi_{0}
of any term in limit, and so we get that it is also an equivalence.

The first isomorphisms in Part 2 follows from
3.22
and identification
A^{D}\simeq A^{\vee}[1]
from
3.33
. The second isomorphism is obtained from Part 1
by applying
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(K(-))
.

For Part 3, note that
\mathbf{L}^{i}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1]))\simeq\colim_{n}\mathbf{L}^{i}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq n}(A[-1]))
, since cohomology commutes with filtered colimits. This way, from
3.34
we get that
\mathbf{L}^{i}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1]))
is
p
-torsion if
i\neq 0
and is the direct sum of
\mathbb Z
and a
p
-torsion abelian group if
i=0
. By the universal coefficients formula, for any
i\in\mathbb Z
we have

0\rightarrow(\mathbf{L}^{i}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1]))/p\rightarrow H^{i}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])\otimes\mathbb F_{p})\rightarrow(\mathbf{L}^{i+1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])[p]\rightarrow 0.

(3.5)

On the other hand, by Part 2 we know that
H^{i}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])\otimes\mathbb F_{p})\simeq 0
for
i\neq 0
. Since a
p
-torsion abelian group
M
is 0 if and only if
M[p]
is we get that
\mathbf{L}^{i}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])\simeq 0
for
i\neq 0,1
. We also get that
(\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1]))/p\simeq 0
, which means that
\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])
is
p
-divisible, and that
(\mathbf{L}^{0}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])[p]\simeq 0
, which means that
(\mathbf{L}^{0}\overline{\mathrm{Bin}}(A[-1])\simeq 0
and
\mathbf{L}^{0}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])\simeq\mathbb Z
.
∎

Remark 3.36
.

For
i=0
by Part 2 of
3.35
we get that short exact sequence
Equation

3.5
takes the form

0\rightarrow\mathbb F_{p}\rightarrow\Map_{{\mathrm{Set}}}(A,\mathbb F_{p})\rightarrow(\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1]))[p]\rightarrow 0,

which gives a non-canonical identification
(\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1]))[p]\simeq\mathbb F_{p}^{\oplus|A|-1}
. Since
\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])
is
p
-divisible, this further gives a non-canonical identification
\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])\simeq(\mathbb Q_{p}/\mathbb Z_{p})^{\oplus|A|-1}
.

Corollary 3.37
.

For any finite abelian group
A\in\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}
we have
\mathbf{L}^{i}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])=0
unless
i=0
or 1.
\mathbf{L}^{0}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])\simeq\mathbb Z
and
\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])
is torsion and divisible.

Proof.

Let
A\simeq\oplus_{p}A_{p}
be the decomposition of
A
as a direct sum of its
p
-primary components. We have an equivalence

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])\simeq\otimes_{p}^{\mathbb L}\ \!\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(A_{p}[-1]).

By the Künneth formula, since
\mathbb Q_{p}/\mathbb Z_{p}\otimes^{\mathbb L}\mathbb Q_{\ell}/\mathbb Z_{\ell}\simeq 0
for any primes
p\neq\ell
, and
3.35
we get that
\mathbf{L}^{i}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])=0
unless
i=0
or 1 and
\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])\simeq\oplus_{p}\ \!\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A_{p}[-1])
, while
\mathbf{L}^{0}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])\simeq\mathbb Z
. The statement follows.
∎

Remark 3.38
.

As in
3.36
, in terms of the decomposition
A\simeq\oplus_{p}A_{p}
one can non-canonically identify

\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])\simeq\oplus_{p}\ \!(\mathbb Q_{p}/\mathbb Z_{p})^{\oplus|A_{p}|-1}.

In
3.42
we will give a more canonical (but less explicit) formula for
\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])
.

Remark 3.39
.

Let
\Phi\coloneqq\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(-[-1])\colon\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{tors}}
. Note that via the Künneth formula, for any
A_{1},A_{2}\in\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}
one gets an identification

\Phi(A_{1}\oplus A_{2})\simeq\Phi(A_{1})\oplus\Phi(A_{2})\oplus\Tor^{1}(\Phi(A_{1}),\Phi(A_{2})).

We see that the functor
\Phi\coloneqq\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(-[-1])\colon\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{tors}}

behaves as the
“torsion multiplicative formal group law”
.

Description of
\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}(A[-1])

Construction 3.40
.

Consider the functor
\Phi^{t}(A)\coloneqq\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}(A[-1])
as a functor

\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ab}
. By the
3.34
it in fact takes values in
\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}\subset\mathrm{Ab}
.
By universal coefficients formula we have a short exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{Ab}}(\Phi^{t}(A),\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}^{0}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}(A[-1]),\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Ab}}(\mathbf{L}^{0}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}A[-1],\mathbb Z)\rightarrow 0.

(3.6)

We shall describe the functor

\Psi^{t}(A)\coloneqq\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}^{0}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}(A[-1]),\mathbb{Z})=H_{0}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}(A[-1])^{\vee})
.
It is a contravariant functor from
\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}
to
\mathrm{Ab}
. By the discussion in
3.33
the left term in
Equation

3.6
is naturally identified with
(\Phi^{t}(A))^{D}
, and so
Equation

3.6
gives a short exact sequence of functors

0\rightarrow(\Phi^{t})^{D}\rightarrow\Psi^{t}\rightarrow\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow 0

from
{({\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}})}^{{\mathrm{op}}}
to
\mathrm{Ab}
. Thus knowing
\Psi^{t}(A)
with the map
\Psi^{t}(A)\rightarrow\mathbb Z
would be enough to reconstruct
\Phi^{t}(A)
.

Theorem 3.41
.

There is a functorial in
A\in\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}
isomorphism

\Psi^{t}(A)\simeq\mathbb{Z}[A^{D}]/J_{A}^{t+1},

where
J_{A}\subset\mathbb{Z}[A^{D}]
is the augmentation ideal.
The natural morphism
\Psi^{t}(A)\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
is identified with
the augmentation.
Consequently, one gets a functorial in
A
isomorphism

\Phi^{t}(A)\simeq(J_{A}/J_{A}^{t+1})^{D}.

Proof.

Pick a free resolution
[L_{0}\xrightarrow{d}L_{1}]\sim A[-1]
.
Recall that by
2.5
for a free abelian group
L
there is
a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}(L)\coloneqq\Map_{\leq t}(L^{\vee},\mathbb Z)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathbb{Z}[L^{\vee}]/J_{L}^{t+1})^{\vee},

where
J_{L}\subset\mathbb{Z}[L^{\vee}]
is the augmentation ideal.
Let
\mathrm{DK}^{\bullet}([L^{0}\xrightarrow{d}L^{1}])\simeq L_{0}\oplus L_{1}^{\oplus\bullet}
denote the cosimplicial abelian group Dold-Kan corresponding to the 2-term complex
[L^{0}\xrightarrow{d}L^{1}]
; we have
A[-1]\simeq\Tot_{[\bullet]\in\Delta}(\mathrm{DK}^{\bullet}([L^{0}\xrightarrow{d}L^{1}]))
. By
2.52
,
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}
commutes with finite totalizations and so we get

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}A[-1]\simeq\Tot_{[\bullet]\in\Delta}\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}\!\left(\mathrm{DK}^{\bullet}([L^{0}\xrightarrow{d}L^{1}])\right)\simeq\Tot_{[\bullet]\in\Delta}\mathrm{Bin}(L_{0}\oplus L_{1}^{\oplus\bullet}),

and then

\Psi^{t}(A)=\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}^{0}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}A[-1],\mathbb{Z})\simeq\coker\left(f_{0}-f_{1}\colon\mathbb{Z}[L_{0}^{\vee}\oplus L_{1}^{\vee}]/J_{L_{0}\oplus L_{1}}^{t+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[L_{0}^{\vee}]/J_{L_{0}}^{t+1}\right),

where the maps
f_{i}
are given by
f_{0}=i^{\vee}_{0}
and
f_{1}=i^{\vee}_{0}\oplus d^{\vee}
correspondingly (here
i_{0}\colon L_{0}\hookrightarrow L_{0}\oplus L_{1}
is the natural embedding and
d^{\vee}\colon L_{1}^{\vee}\rightarrow L_{0}^{\vee}
is dual to the differential
d\colon L_{0}\rightarrow L_{1}
). Both
f_{0}
and
f_{1}

are homomorphisms of algebras.
Let
u\in L_{0}^{\vee}
and
v\in L_{1}^{\vee}
, and consider
the basis element
[u]\cdot[v]\in\mathbb{Z}[L_{0}^{\vee}\oplus L_{1}^{\vee}]
.
By definition,
f_{0}([u]\cdot[v])=[u]
and
f_{1}([u]\cdot[v])=[u]\cdot[d^{\vee}(v)]
.
We see that
\mathrm{Im}(f_{0}-f_{1})\subset\mathbb{Z}[L_{0}^{\vee}]/J_{L_{0}}^{t+1}
is an ideal generated by

1-[d^{\vee}(v)]
for all
v\in L_{1}^{\vee}\subset\mathbb{Z}[L_{1}^{\vee}]
.
This gives

\Psi^{t}(A)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{Z}[L_{0}^{\vee}]/(J_{L_{0}}^{t+1},1-[d^{\vee}(v)])_{v\in L_{1}^{\vee}}\simeq\mathbb{Z}[L_{0}^{\vee}/L_{1}^{\vee}]/J_{L_{0}}^{t+1}.

Finally, noting that
L_{0}^{\vee}/L_{1}^{\vee}\simeq\Ext^{1}(A,\mathbb Z)\simeq A^{D}
we get the first statement.
From the proof above one also sees that the augmentation map

\mathbb{Z}[A^{D}]/J_{A}^{t+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}

is exactly induced by the restriction

\mathrm{Hom}^{0}_{{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}(A[-1]),\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Ab}}(\mathbf{L}^{0}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}(A[-1]),\mathbb{Z})=\mathbb{Z}.

Thus by
Equation

3.6
we can identify

\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{Ab}}(\Phi^{t}(A),\mathbb{Z})\simeq\ker(\mathbb{Z}[A^{D}]/J_{A}^{t+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z})\simeq J_{A}/J_{A}^{t+1},

and then, passing to Pontryagin duals again, also

\Phi^{t}(A)\simeq\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{Ab}}(\Phi^{t}(A),\mathbb{Z})^{D}\simeq(J_{A}/J_{A}^{t+1})^{D}.

∎

Corollary 3.42
.

Let
A\in\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fin}}
. Then there is a natural in
A
isomorphism

\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A[-1])\simeq\colim_{t\geq 1}(J_{A}/J_{A}^{t+1})^{D},

where
J_{A}\subset\mathbb Z[A^{D}]
is the augmentation ideal.

Proof.

Since cohomology commutes with filtered colimits, by
3.41
we get

\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(A)[-1]\simeq\colim_{t\geq 1}\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}(A)[-1]\simeq\colim_{t\geq 1}(J_{A}/J_{A}^{t+1})^{D}.

∎

In the case
A=\mathbb{Z}/p
we get a particularly nice description.
Let
K\coloneqq\mathbb Q_{p}(\zeta_{p})
where
\zeta_{p}^{p}=1
is a primitive root of unity and let
\mathcal{O}_{K}\subset K
denote the ring of integers. The element
\pi\coloneqq\zeta_{p}-1
is a uniformizer of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Also note that
\Aut_{\mathrm{Ab}}(\mathbb Z/p)\simeq(\mathbb Z/p)^{\times}
and that
(\mathbb Z/p)^{\times}\simeq\Gal(K/\mathbb Q_{p})
.

Corollary 3.43
.

There is an isomorphism

\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}(\mathbb{Z}/p[-1])\simeq((\pi)/(\pi^{t+1}))^{D}

that is compatible with the filtrations and the action
of
(\mathbb Z/p)^{\times}
.

Proof.

Let
L\coloneqq\mathbb Z[(\mathbb Z/p)^{D}]
; we can
(\mathbb Z/p)^{\times}
-equivariantly identify
L
with
\mathbb Z[x]/(x^{p}-1)
, where
i\in(\mathbb Z/p)^{\times}
sends
x
to
x^{i}
. By
3.41

\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}(\mathbb{Z}/p[-1])
is identified with the Pontryagin dual of the quotient
(x-1)L/(x-1)^{t+1}L
. By
3.34
this is a finite
p
-torsion group, so

(x-1)L/(x-1)^{t+1}L\simeq((x-1)L/(x-1)^{t+1}L)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Z_{p}\simeq(x-1)L^{\wedge}_{p}/(x-1)^{t+1}L^{\wedge}_{p},

where
L^{\wedge}_{p}\coloneqq\mathbb Z_{p}[(\mathbb Z/p)^{D}]
. Let
\varphi_{p}(x)\coloneqq x^{p-1}+x^{p-2}+\ldots+1
. We have
x^{p}-1=(x-1)\cdot\varphi_{p}(x)
, and so ideal
(x-1)L^{\wedge}_{p}
has the
\mathcal{O}_{K}\simeq\mathbb Z_{p}[x]/\varphi_{p}(x)
-module structure; moreover the corresponding module is naturally identified with the ideal generated by
(x-1)\in\mathbb Z_{p}[x]/\varphi_{p}(x)
, which corresponds to
\pi\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
. This gives the required isomorphism.
∎

Remark 3.44

(Case
A=\mathbb Z/p
)
.

Let
K/\mathbb Q_{p}
be a finite extension and
\mathfrak{d}_{K/\mathbb Q_{p}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
the different ideal of
K
; recall that by definition the inverse ideal
\mathfrak{d}_{K/\mathbb Q_{p}}^{-1}\subset K
is identified with the dual
\mathbb Z_{p}
-lattice to
\mathcal{O}_{K}
via the pairing
(\alpha,\beta)\mapsto\tr_{K/\mathbb Q_{p}}(\alpha\beta)
. The Pontryagin dual
(\mathcal{O}_{K})^{D}
(defined, say, as the group of continuous homomorphisms
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Ab}}^{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathcal{O}_{K},\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)
) is
\Gal(K/\mathbb Q_{p})
-equivariantly identified with
K/\mathfrak{d}_{K/\mathbb Q_{p}}^{-1}
via sending
\alpha\in K/\mathfrak{d}_{K/\mathbb Q_{p}}^{-1}
to the homomorphism
\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{K}\mapsto\tr_{K/\mathbb Q_{p}}(\alpha\beta)
, where the latter is a well-defined element of
\mathbb Q_{p}/\mathbb Z_{p}\subset\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z
. More generally, for any
x,y\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
, such that
x|y
, this restricts to a
\Gal(K/\mathbb Q_{p})
-equivariant isomorphism

((y)/(x))^{D}\simeq(x\cdot\mathfrak{d}_{K/\mathbb Q_{p}})^{-1}/(y\cdot\mathfrak{d}_{K/\mathbb Q_{p}})^{-1}

In the case
K=\mathbb Q_{p}(\zeta_{p})
the different ideal is given by
(\zeta_{p}-1)^{p-2}=(\pi^{p-2})\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
. By
3.43
we get an isomorphism

\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}^{\leq t}(\mathbb{Z}/p[-1])\simeq((\pi)/(\pi^{t+1}))^{D}\simeq(\pi^{-t-1-p})/(\pi^{1-p})\simeq(\pi^{-t})/\mathcal{O}_{K}

Here in the last isomorphism we used the equality of ideals
(\zeta_{p}-1)^{p-1}=(p)
, which follows from the fact that
\mathrm{val}_{p}(\zeta_{p}-1)=1/p-1
. By passing to the limit as
t\rightarrow\infty
we also get a
(\mathbb Z/p)^{\times}
-equivariant isomorphism

\mathbf{L}^{1}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb{Z}/p[-1])\simeq K/\mathcal{O}_{K},

where
(\mathbb Z/p)^{\times}
-acts on the right through the Galois action.

Remark 3.45
.

Note that
\mathbb Z/p[-1]
is equivalent to a (finite) totalization of the Dold-Kan corresponding cosimplicial object
\mathrm{DK}^{\bullet}(\mathbb Z/p[-1])\simeq\mathbb Z/p^{\oplus\bullet}
:

\mathbb Z/p[-1]\xrightarrow{\sim}\Tot_{[\bullet]\in\Delta}\mathbb Z/p^{\oplus\bullet}.

By
3.30
,
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z/p^{\oplus\bullet})\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(0)\simeq\mathbb Z
, and so

\Tot_{[\bullet]\in\Delta}\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z/p^{\oplus\bullet})\simeq\Tot_{[\bullet]\in\Delta}\mathbb Z\simeq\mathbb Z,

which does not agree with
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z/p[-1])
. This shows that
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
does not commute with finite totalizations if we even slightly relax the boundedness condition in
2.70
(note that even though
\mathbb Z/p^{\oplus\bullet}
is coconnective it does not lie in
\Ind((\Perf_{\mathbb Z}^{\leq 0})^{\vee})
).

4
Derived binomial algebras and cohomology of log analytic spaces

In this section we apply the derived binomial monad
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
to give a formula for integral Betti(=singular) cohomology of fs log analytic spaces. The latter was defined in
[
KN99
, Definition 1.1.1]
in terms of an auxiliary topological space
\pi\colon X^{\log}\rightarrow X
which now carries the name of
“Kato-Nakayama space”
. In
Section

4.1
we recall its definition and also introduce the 2-term complex
\eexp(\alpha)\coloneqq\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\gr}
, which gives an explicit model for
R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
. In
Section

4.2
, for a sufficiently nice topological space
X
we construct the derived binomial monad
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}
on the category
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{D}(\mathbb Z))
of
\mathscr{D}(\mathbb Z)
-valued sheaves on
X
and identify modules over
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}
with the category of sheaves of derived binomial rings on
X
. In
Section

4.3
we define a slightly modified version
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in^{\mathrm{coaug}}_{X}
of a free binomial ring in the undercategory
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{D}(\mathbb Z))_{\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}
. In
Section

4.4
we then show that given a proper map
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
of sufficiently nice topological spaces such that each fiber is a compact real torus, the pushforward
R\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
is the free binomial ring
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in^{\mathrm{coaug}}_{X}(R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z})
; applying this to
\pi\colon X^{\log}\rightarrow X
gives a formula for
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{sing}}^{\log}(X,\mathbb Z)
as global sections of
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in^{\mathrm{coaug}}_{X}(\eexp(\alpha))
. In
Section

4.5
we show that one can also reconstruct from
\eexp(\alpha)
the cosheaf
[X^{\log},\pi]
represented by
X^{\log}
and also define a gerbe structure on the latter over the cosheaf of groups
\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}}^{\vee}
. Finally, in
Section

4.6
we propose an algebraic version
X^{\log}_{\mathbb H}
of
[X^{\log},\pi]
which we hope to be able to generalize to other cohomological contexts in the future.

4.1
Log singular cohomology

Definition 4.1

(
[
KN99
, Definition 1.1.1]
)
.

Recall that a log complex analytic space
(X,\mathcal{M})
is given by:

-

a complex analytic space
X
;

-

a sheaf of commutative monoids
\mathcal{M}
;

-

a homomorphism
16
16

16

Here we consider the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X}
of holomorphic functions as a sheaf of monoids with the operation given by multiplication.

\alpha\colon\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}
such that
\alpha^{-1}(\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{X})\simeq\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{X}
.

Assumption 4.2
.

In this paper we will only consider
fine and saturated (fs)
log analytic spaces (
[
KN99
, Definition 1.1.2]
). The key property of fs log analytic spaces is that any fiber of the sheaf
\mathcal{M}
is a fine and saturated monoid (see
[
Ogu18
, Section I.1.3]
). In particular, the
ghost sheaf

\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}}:=\coker(\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}^{\gr})
(where
\mathcal{M}^{\gr}
denotes the group completion of a sheaf of monoids) is constructible with stalks given by torsion-free finitely generated abelian groups (in fact this is the only property of fs log structure that we are going to use).

The classical approach to defining log Betti (=singular) cohomology of a log analytic space is via the construction of the corresponding Kato-Nakayama space
X^{\log}
(
[
KN99
]
), that we now recall.

Construction 4.3

(Kato-Nakayama space)
.

To an fs log analytic space
(X,\mathcal{M})
one can associate a certain natural topological space
X^{\log}
, together with a continuous map
X^{\log}\rightarrow X
. The space
X^{\log}
is called the
Kato-Nakayama space
of
(X,\mathcal{M})
. The underlying set of
X^{\log}
is given by the pairs
(x,\varphi)
of a point
x\in X
, and a group homomorphism

\varphi\colon\mathcal{M}^{\gr}_{x}\rightarrow S^{1}

that makes the diagram

\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}_{X,x}^{\times}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{arg}_{x}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}_{x}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{S^{1}}

commutative. Here
\arg_{x}
denotes the
argument
function that sends

f\mapsto\arg_{x}(f)\coloneqq\frac{f(x)}{|f(x)|}\in S^{1}\subset\mathbb C.

The topology on
X^{\log}
is defined as follows. By construction, we have a natural map of sets
\pi\colon X^{\log}\rightarrow X
, sending
(x,\varphi)
to
x
. For any open
U\subset X
and a section
{m}\in\mathcal{M}(U)
,
we can define a function

\mathrm{arg}({m})\colon\pi^{-1}(U)\longrightarrow S^{1};

(x,\varphi)\longmapsto\varphi({m}_{x}).

The space
X^{log}
is endowed with the weak topology defined
by
\pi
and maps
\mathrm{arg}({m})
for all
m\in\mathcal{M}^{\gr}_{{\mathrm{an}}}(U)
and open
U\subset X^{an}
.

Remark 4.4
.

By
[
Ogu18
, Corollary V.1.2.8]
the map
\pi\colon X^{\log}\rightarrow X
is proper. The fiber
\pi^{-1}(x)\hookrightarrow X^{\log}
over a point
x\in X
is identified with the set of group homomorphisms

\pi^{-1}(x)\simeq\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}_{x}},S^{1}),

with the topology induced from
S^{1}
. In particular,
\pi^{-1}(x)
is a compact real torus, whose dimension is equal to the rank of
\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}_{x}}
.

Definition 4.5

(Log singular cohomology)
.

The
log Betti cohomology
17
17

17

Or, in other words, singular log cohomology.

R\Gamma^{\log}_{{\mathrm{sing}}}(X,\mathbb Z)
of a log analytic space
X
is defined as the the singular cohomology
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{sing}}(X^{\log},\mathbb Z)
of the topological space
X^{\log}
.

Remark 4.6

(Log-schemes over
\mathbb C
and analytification)
.

To an
fs log scheme

(X,\mathcal{M})
(
[
Nak97
, Definition 1.7]
) over
\mathbb C
with
X
being of finite type one can naturally associate its
analytification

(X_{{\mathrm{an}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathrm{an}}})
(where
\mathcal{M}_{{\mathrm{an}}}
is simply defined by taking pull-back of the log-structure from
X
to
X_{\mathrm{an}}
) which is an fs log analytic space. Following
[
KN99
]
one can define log Betti cohomology of
(X,\mathcal{M})
as log Betti cohomology of
(X_{{\mathrm{an}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathrm{an}}})
. As shown in
[
KN99
]
, it agrees with the appropriately defined log étale and log de Rham cohomology under naturally defined comparison isomorphisms.

As discussed in the introduction, one can look for alternative, more direct descriptions of the log-Betti cohomology that would only directly involve the analytic space
X
and the monoid
\mathcal{M}
. Let
(X,\mathcal{M})
be an fs log analytic space. We will denote by

\exp_{\mathcal{M}}\colon\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{}\mathcal{M}^{\gr}

the composition of the classical exponential map
\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{\exp}\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{X}
with the natural inclusion
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\gr}
given by the log structure.

Notation 4.7

(2-term complex of sheaves
\eexp(\alpha)
)
.

Let

\eexp(\alpha)\coloneqq\fib(\exp_{\mathcal{M}}\colon\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\gr})

be the fiber of
\exp_{\mathcal{M}}
in the category
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
of sheaves of abelian groups on
X
. Equivalently, we consider an object of
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
represented by the 2-term complex
[\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\gr}]
. The cohomology sheaves of
\eexp(\alpha)
are given by
H^{0}(\eexp(\alpha))\simeq\ker(\exp_{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\ker(\exp)\simeq\underline{\mathbb Z}
and
H^{1}(\eexp(\alpha))\simeq\coker(\exp_{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}}\coloneqq\mathcal{M}^{\gr}/\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times}
.

Remark 4.8
.

As we will see below, the complex
\eexp(\alpha)
is an explicit model for
R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
, where
\pi\colon X^{\log}\rightarrow X
is the natural projection from the Kato-Nakayama space (
4.36
).

To compute
R\Gamma^{\log}_{\mathrm{Betti}}(X,\mathbb Z)
one can proceed in two steps: namely, first take pushforward
R\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
to
X
and then compute the derived global sections of the latter. Our goal will be to give a closed formula for the full derived pushforward
R\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
in terms of its first truncation
R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}\simeq\eexp(\alpha)
.

4.2
Sheaves of binomial rings

Notation 4.9
.

Let
X
be a topological space and let
\mathrm{Op}(X)
be the category given by open subsets of
X
. Recall that a
sieve
on
X
is a collection
\mathfrak{U}
of open subsets of
X
, such that if
V\in\mathfrak{U}
and
V^{\prime}\subset V
, then
V^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{U}
. If
U\simeq\cup_{V\in\mathfrak{U}}V
we will say that
\mathfrak{U}
is a covering sieve for
U
. The data
U\mapsto\{\mathfrak{U},\text{ $\mathfrak{U}$ covers $U$}\}
gives rise to an
(\infty,1)
-site in the sense of
[
Lur09
, Definition 6.2.2.1]
. For an
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
the category of
\mathscr{C}
-valued presheaves is simply defined as the functor category
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\coloneqq\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathrm{Op}(X)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{C})
. Assume
\mathscr{C}
has all small limits. Then a presheaf
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})
is called a sheaf if for any covering sieve
\mathfrak{U}
of
U
the natural map

\mathcal{F}(U)\rightarrow\lim_{V\in\mathfrak{U}}\mathcal{F}(V)

is an equivalence. We will denote by
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\subset{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\coloneqq\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathrm{Op}(X)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{C})
the
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{C}
-valued sheaves on
X
.

By
[
Lur18
, Remark 1.3.1.6]
, if
\mathscr{C}
is presentable,
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})
can be identified with the Lurie’s tensor product
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})\otimes\mathscr{C}
, and is also presentable. Also, by
[
Lur18
, Lemma 1.3.4.3]
, the inclusion
i_{X}\colon{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})
has a left adjoint
\#_{X}\colon{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})\to{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
, which we will call the sheafification functor.

Remark 4.10
.

Recall that a paracompact topological space
X
is said to have
covering dimension
\leq n
if the following condition is satisfied: for any open covering

\{U_{\alpha}\}
of
X
, there exists an open refinement
\{V_{\beta}\}
such that each intersection
V_{\beta_{0}}\cap\ldots\cap V_{\beta_{n+1}}=\varnothing
provided the
\beta_{i}
are pairwise distinct. By
[
Lur09
, Theorem 7.2.3.6]
if
X
is paracompact and has locally finite covering dimension, then the
\infty
-topos
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}_{\infty}(X)\coloneqq{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
has locally finite
homotopy dimension
. This implies some nice properties of
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}_{\infty}(X)
: most importantly Postnikov towers are convergent
18
18

18

Meaning that any
F\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}_{\infty}(X)
the natural map
F\rightarrow\lim_{n}\tau^{\leq n}F
is an equivalence.

[
Lur09
, Proposition 7.2.1.10]
and, consequently,
\infty
-topos
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}_{\infty}(X)
is hypercomplete; in other words,
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}_{\infty}(X)\simeq\mathrm{h}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
, where
\mathrm{h}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}
denotes the category of hypersheaves. Note that this also implies that
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\simeq\mathrm{h}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})
for any presentable
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
. By
[
Lur09
, Corollary 7.2.1.17]
it also follows that a map
p\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})
is an equivalence if and only if the map of stalks
p_{x}\colon\mathcal{F}_{x}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{x}
in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(\mathrm{pt},\mathscr{C})\simeq\mathscr{C}
is an equivalence for all
x\in X
.

Assumption 4.11
.

Below we are going to assume that all topological spaces are paracompact locally of finite covering dimension.
We note that any locally closed subspace of
\mathbb R^{n}
has a covering dimension
\leq n
; in particular any complex analytic space
X
, or Kato-Nakayama space
X^{\log}
corresponding to some fs log structure
\mathcal{M}
are paracompact of locally finite covering dimension.

Remark 4.12

(
t
-structure)
.

We will be primarily working with the category
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
of
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
-valued sheaves. By
[
Lur18
, Proposition 2.1.1.1]
it has a natural
t
-structure
({\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))^{\leq 0},{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))^{\geq 0})
with the subcategory
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))^{\geq 0}
being spanned by those
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
such that
\mathcal{F}(U)\in{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\geq 0}
for any
U
. The heart
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))^{\heartsuit}\subset{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
is naturally identified with the abelian category
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathrm{Ab})
of classical sheaves of abelian groups on
X
.

We note that the image of
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathrm{Ab})
under the resulting functor
19
19

19

Note that the functor
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathrm{Ab})\rightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{D}(\mathbb Z))
does not preserve the subcategories of sheaves. The functor in question is obtained as the composition of the natural functor
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathrm{Ab})\rightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{D}(\mathbb Z))
and sheafification
\#_{X}\colon{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{D}(\mathbb Z))\to{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{D}(\mathbb Z))
.

{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathrm{Ab})\simeq{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))^{\heartsuit}\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))

is sending an open
U
to
R\Gamma(U,\mathcal{F})
rather than
\mathcal{F}(U)\in\mathrm{Ab}\subset D(\mathbb Z)
.

Remark 4.13

(Comparison with the derived category of sheaves)
.

Identification
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathrm{Ab})\simeq{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))^{\heartsuit}
extends to a fully faithful functor

\mathscr{D}^{+}\!({\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathrm{Ab}))\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)).

If the
\infty
-topos
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
is hypercomplete, the
t
-structure on
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
is left-separated, and by
[
Lur18
, Section 2.1]
the latter functor extends to an equivalence

\mathscr{D}({\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathrm{Ab}))\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))

with the unbounded derived category
\mathscr{D}({\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathrm{Ab}))
.

The monad
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
on
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
naturally extends to a monad on the category of presheaves
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))\coloneqq\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathrm{Op}(X)^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
via post-composition with
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
. We will denote this monad by
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}
. Via Barr-Beck-Lurie theorem one can identify the category of modules over
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}
in
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
with the category of presheaves
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg)
on
X
with values in
\DBinAlg
.

Let us as before denote by
i_{X}
the inclusion
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
, and by
\#_{X}
its left adjoint
“sheafification”
functor
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))\to{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
. Note that since
i_{X}
is fully faithful, by definition,
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
is a reflective localization of
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
. We can define an endofunctor of
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
by the formula:

\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}\coloneqq\#_{X}\circ\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}\circ i_{X}.

Proposition 4.14

(
[
Rak20
, Proposition 4.1.9.]
)
.

Let
\tau\colon\mathscr{C}\rightleftarrows\mathscr{C}_{0}:\!i
be a localization of
\infty
-categories. Let
\mathrm{T}
be a monad on
\mathscr{C}
such that the unit transformation
\mathrm{id}_{\mathscr{C}}\to i\circ\tau
induces an equivalence
\tau\circ\mathrm{T}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\tau\circ\mathrm{T}\circ i\circ\tau
. Then there is an induced monad structure on the composite
\mathrm{T}_{0}\coloneqq\tau\circ\mathrm{T}\circ i
and an induced localization

\tau\colon\mathrm{L}\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathscr{C})\rightleftarrows\mathrm{L}\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}_{\mathrm{T}_{0}}(\mathscr{C}_{0})\ \!\!:\!i

where the embedding
i
identifies
\mathrm{L}\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}_{\mathrm{T}_{0}}(\mathscr{C}_{0})
with the fiber product
\mathrm{L}\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}_{\mathrm{T}}(\mathscr{C})\times_{\mathscr{C}}\mathscr{C}_{0}
.

Corollary 4.15
.

The endofunctor
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}\mathrm{in}_{X}\coloneqq\#_{X}\circ\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}\circ i_{X}
has a natural structure of a monad.

Proof.

We apply
4.14
.
Using adjunction
\#_{X}\dashv i_{X}
, we may consider
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
as the reflective localization of
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
. Let
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
be a presheaf. Unit of the adjunction
\#_{X}\dashv i_{X}
induces the map

(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}(\mathcal{F}))^{\#}\overset{\varphi}{\longrightarrow}(\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}(\mathcal{F}^{\#}))^{\#},

and we only need to check that
\varphi
is an equivalence. By our assumptions on
X
, it is enough to check that it induces an equivalence on all stalks
\varphi_{x}
at all points
x\in X
. Note that the sheafification does not change the stalks, so we can as well replace
\varphi_{x}
with the map

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}(\eta)_{x}\colon\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}(\mathcal{F})_{x}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}(\mathcal{F}^{\#})_{x},

where
\eta\colon F\to F^{\#}
is the unit of
\#_{X}\dashv i_{X}
adjunction. But, since the stalk is given by a filtered colimit, and
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
commutes with filtered colimits, this map can be identified with the identity map
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathcal{F}_{x})\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}((\mathcal{F}^{\#})_{x})\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\mathcal{F}_{x})
.
∎

Notation 4.16

(Derived binomial rings over
X
)
.

We will denote by

\DBinAlg(X)\coloneqq\mathrm{L}\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}_{\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}}({\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)))

the
\infty
-category of modules over
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}
in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
. Slightly abusing notation, for
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
we will now denote by
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\mathcal{F})\in\DBinAlg(X)
the free derived binomial ring on
\mathcal{F}
, as well as its underlying object in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
.

Example 4.17
.

Let
X\coloneqq\mathrm{pt}
be a point. In this case
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(\mathrm{pt},{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))\simeq{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(\mathrm{pt},{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))\simeq{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
and
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{\mathrm{pt}}\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{\mathrm{pt}}\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
; we get an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\DBinAlg(\mathrm{pt})\simeq\DBinAlg.

Remark 4.18
.

Since
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
commutes with sifted colimits, and colimits in
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
are computed pointwise, we see that
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}
also commutes with sifted colimits. We claim that
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}\mathrm{in}_{X}
commutes with sifted colimits as well. Indeed, given a sifted diagram
I
and a functor
I\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
,
i\mapsto\mathcal{F}_{i}

it suffices to show that the canonical map

\colim_{i\in I}\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}\mathrm{in}_{X}(\mathcal{F}_{i})\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}\mathrm{in}_{X}(\colim_{i\in I}\mathcal{F}_{i})

is a quasi-isomorphism. Again, this can be checked on the level of stalks. Here we have

\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}\mathrm{in}_{X}(-)_{x}\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}(-)_{x}\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}((-)_{x})

so we can rewrite the map above as

\colim_{i\in I}\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}((\mathcal{F}_{i})_{x})\rightarrow\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(\colim_{i\in I}((\mathcal{F}_{i})_{x})).

and use that
\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}
commutes with sifted colimits.
As a corollary, by
[
Rak20
, Proposition 4.1.10]
we get that the categories
\mathrm{LMod}_{\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}}({\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
and
\DBinAlg(X)
are presentable. In particular, both categories admit all small limits and colimits.

By
[
Lur17
, Corollary 4.2.3.5]
it follows that the forgetful functor

U_{X}\colon\DBinAlg(X)\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))

commutes with sifted colimits.

Remark 4.19
.

By

4.14
and the natural equivalence

\mathrm{L}\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}_{\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}_{X}}({\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)))\simeq{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg)

we get another natural equivalence

\DBinAlg(X)\simeq{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg)\times_{{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)).

(4.1)

In other words, the category
\DBinAlg(X)
is given by the full subcategory in
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg)
of presheaves of derived binomial rings on
X
whose underlying complex is a sheaf. Since the forgetful functor
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg)\rightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
is conservative and commutes with limits, we get an identification of
\DBinAlg(X)
with the category of
\DBinAlg
-valued sheaves on
X
:

\DBinAlg(X)\simeq{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg).

We will denote by

\#_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\colon{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg)\rightleftarrows\DBinAlg(X)\ \!\!:\!i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}

the associated sheafification functor and its right adjoint. We might occasionally omit the superscript
“
\mathrm{Bin}
”
to simplify notation.

Let us also note that via the identification in
4.14
as the fiber product, identifying two projections, we get a natural equivalence

U_{X}^{\mathrm{psh}}\circ i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\simeq i_{X}\circ U_{X},

where we denoted by
U_{X}^{\mathrm{psh}}\colon{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg)\rightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
the post-composition with the forgetful functor
U\colon\DBinAlg\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
.

Lemma 4.20
.

Let
X
be as in
4.11
. There is a natural equivalence of functors

\#_{X}\circ U_{X}^{\mathrm{psh}}\xrightarrow{\sim}U_{X}\circ\#_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}

from
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg)
to
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))

Proof.

From
U_{X}^{\mathrm{psh}}\circ i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\simeq i_{X}\circ U_{X}
and
\#_{X}\dashv i_{X}
adjunction, one gets a natural transformation
\#_{X}\circ U_{X}^{\mathrm{psh}}\circ i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\rightarrow U_{X}
. Pre-composing it with
\#_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}
, and using
\#_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\dashv i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}
adjunction we get transformations

\#_{X}\circ U_{X}^{\mathrm{psh}}\rightarrow\#_{X}\circ U_{X}^{\mathrm{psh}}\circ i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\circ\#_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\rightarrow U_{X}\circ\#_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}},

giving the required map as the composition. Moreover, none of the two functors change the stalks (or rather their underlying objects in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
), and the induced map on stalks is the identity map, so this transformation is an equivalence.
∎

The next step is to define direct and inverse image functors for
\DBinAlg(-)
. We will do so by sheafifying the corresponding functors on the level of presheaves.

Remark 4.21

(Pull-back and pushforward for presheaves)
.

Recall that for any cocomplete category
\mathscr{C}
, the pull-back
f^{\dagger}_{\mathscr{C}}\colon{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\rightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(Y,\mathscr{C})
on the categories of
\mathscr{C}
-valued presheaves is defined as the left adjoint to the functor
Rf_{\dagger,\mathscr{C}}\colon{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(Y,\mathscr{C})\rightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})
obtained by pre-composition with
f^{-1,{\mathrm{op}}}\colon\mathrm{Op}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}(Y)^{\mathrm{op}}
. Any functor
F\colon\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
induces functors

F_{X}\colon{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\rightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{D}),\qquad F_{Y}\colon{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(Y,\mathscr{C})\rightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(Y,\mathscr{D})

via post-composition with
F
, and we have a natural equivalence
Rf_{\dagger,\mathscr{D}}\circ F_{Y}\simeq F_{X}\circ Rf_{\dagger,\mathscr{C}}
of functors
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(Y,\mathscr{C})\rightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{D})
. If
F
has a left adjoint
G
we also get a natural equivalence

G_{Y}\circ f^{\dagger}_{\mathscr{D}}\simeq f^{\dagger}_{\mathscr{C}}\circ G_{X}.

Note that since
f^{-1}\colon\mathrm{Op}(X)\rightarrow\mathrm{Op}(Y)
sends open covers to open covers,
Rf_{\dagger,\mathrm{Bin}}\colon{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(Y,\DBinAlg)\rightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg)
sends sheaves to sheaves.

Construction 4.22

(
Rf_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}
and
f^{*}_{\mathrm{Bin}}
)
.

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a continuous map of topological spaces satisfying
4.11
. We define the
(derived) pushforward functor

Rf_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}\colon\DBinAlg(Y)\rightarrow\DBinAlg(X)

as the restriction of
Rf_{\dagger,\mathrm{Bin}}
to
\DBinAlg(Y)\subset{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(Y,\DBinAlg)
. Note that
Rf_{\dagger,\mathrm{Bin}}
commutes with all small limits, and since limits of sheaves can be computed on the level of presheaves, so does
Rf_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}
. We define the
pull-back functor

f_{\mathrm{Bin}}^{*}\colon\DBinAlg(X)\rightarrow\DBinAlg(Y)

as the left adjoint to
Rf_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}
, that exists by Lurie’s adjoint functor theorem. Being a left adjoint,
f_{\mathrm{Bin}}^{*}
commutes with all small colimits.

Remark 4.23
.

The chain of natural equivalences

\Map(f^{*}_{\mathrm{Bin}}\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})\simeq\Map(\mathcal{A},Rf_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}\mathcal{B})\simeq\Map(i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\mathcal{A},i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}(Rf_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}\mathcal{B}))\simeq

\simeq\Map(i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\mathcal{A},Rf_{\dagger,\mathrm{Bin}}(i_{Y}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\mathcal{B}))=\Map(f^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{Bin}}(i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\mathcal{A}),i_{Y}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\mathcal{B})\simeq\Map((\#^{\mathrm{Bin}}_{Y}\circ f^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{Bin}}\circ i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}})(\mathcal{A}),\mathcal{B})

shows that there is a natural equivalence of functors

f^{*}_{\mathrm{Bin}}\simeq\#^{\mathrm{Bin}}_{Y}\circ f^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{Bin}}\circ i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}.

In other words,
f^{*}_{\mathrm{Bin}}
is obtained from the presheaf pull-back
f^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{Bin}}
by sheafification.

Let us now show some basic properties of how the functors
Rf_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}
and
f^{*}_{\mathrm{Bin}}
interact with
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{-}
and
U_{-}
.

Proposition 4.24
.

Let
f\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a continuous map of topological spaces satisfying
4.11
.
There are the following natural equivalences:

f^{*}_{\mathrm{Bin}}\circ\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{Y}\circ f^{*};\,\,\,\,U_{X}\circ Rf_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}\simeq Rf_{*}\circ U_{Y};\,\,\,\,f^{*}\circ U_{X}\simeq U_{Y}\circ f_{\mathrm{Bin}}^{*}.

Proof.

By the discussion in
4.21
applied to
\mathscr{C}\coloneqq\DBinAlg
,
\mathscr{D}\coloneqq{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
,
F\coloneqq U:\DBinAlg\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
we have a natural equivalence of functors

U_{X}^{\mathrm{psh}}\circ Rf_{\dagger,\mathrm{Bin}}\simeq Rf_{\dagger}\circ U_{Y}^{\mathrm{psh}}.

(4.2)

We also have equivalences

\#_{X}\circ U_{X}^{\mathrm{psh}}\circ Rf_{\dagger,\mathrm{Bin}}\circ i_{Y}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\simeq U_{X}\circ\#_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\circ Rf_{\dagger,\mathrm{Bin}}\circ i_{Y}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\simeq U_{Y}\circ Rf_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}

by
4.20
and the fact that
Rf_{\dagger,\mathrm{Bin}}
commutes with
i_{X}
. On the other hand

\#_{X}\circ Rf_{\dagger,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}\circ U_{Y}^{\mathrm{psh}}\circ i_{Y}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\simeq\#_{X}\circ Rf_{\dagger,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)}\circ i_{Y}\circ U_{Y}\simeq Rf_{*}\circ U_{Y},

since
U_{Y}^{\mathrm{psh}}\circ i_{Y}^{\mathrm{Bin}}\simeq i_{Y}\circ U_{Y}
. This way we get the second equivalence, by pre-composing
Equation

4.2
with
i_{Y}^{\mathrm{Bin}}
and post-composing with
\#_{Y}
. The first equivalence then is obtained by taking left adjoints.

Let us now construct the third equivalence.
Using
(\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}\mathrm{in}_{Y})\dashv(U_{Y})
adjunction, the category of transformations
f^{*}\circ U_{X}\Rightarrow U_{Y}\circ f_{\mathrm{Bin}}^{*}
is equivalent to the category of transformations
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}\mathrm{in}_{Y}\circ f^{*}\circ U_{X}\Rightarrow f_{\mathrm{Bin}}^{*}
. Via the natural equivalence
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}\mathrm{in}_{Y}\circ f^{*}\simeq f^{*}_{\mathrm{Bin}}\circ\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}\mathrm{in}_{X}
and counit
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}\mathrm{in}_{X}\circ U_{X}\rightarrow{\mathrm{id}}
of the adjunction we get a transformation
f^{*}\circ U_{X}\rightarrow U_{Y}\circ f_{\mathrm{Bin}}^{*}
. Since the target category is stable, it is enough to check that it is an equivalence when evaluated on any object (of the source category). For
\mathcal{A}\in\DBinAlg(X)
,
(f^{*}\circ U_{X})(\mathcal{A})\simeq(\#_{Y}\circ f^{\dagger}\circ U_{X}^{\mathrm{psf}}\circ i_{X}^{\mathrm{Bin}})(\mathcal{A})
, thus
(f^{*}\circ U_{X})(\mathcal{A})
is the sheafification of the presheaf

W\mapsto\colim_{f(W)\subset V}U(\mathcal{A}(V)).

Similarly, using
4.20
one sees that
(U_{Y}\circ f_{\mathrm{Bin}}^{*})(\mathcal{A})
is obtained as the sheafification of the functor

W\mapsto U\circ\colim_{f(W)\subset V}\mathcal{A}(V),

and that the transformation above is induced by the natural maps

\colim_{f(W)\subset V}U(\mathcal{A}(V))\rightarrow U\circ\colim_{f(W)\subset V}\mathcal{A}(V),

for
W\in\mathrm{Op}(Y)
(here the colimit on the left is computed in
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
, while the colimit on the right is in
\DBinAlg
).
Since
U
commutes with filtered colimits these maps are equivalences, and thus
f^{*}\circ U_{X}\simeq U_{Y}\circ f_{\mathrm{Bin}}^{*}
.

∎

Remark 4.25
.

4.24
shows in particular that functors
f^{*}_{\mathrm{Bin}}
and
Rf_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}
are compatible with functors
f^{*}
and
Rf_{*}
for
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
-valued sheaves, in the sense that their underlying objects in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(-,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
are computed by the latter.

Example 4.26
.

(Constant derived binomial rings)
Let
X
be a topological space satisfying
4.11
and let
p_{X}\colon X\rightarrow\mathrm{pt}
be the unique map. Given a derived binomial ring
A\in\DBinAlg\simeq\DBinAlg(\mathrm{pt})
we define the
constant binomial ring

\underline{A}\in\DBinAlg(X)
as the pull-back
\underline{A}\coloneqq p_{X,\mathrm{Bin}}^{*}A
. By
4.23
,
\underline{A}
can be also seen as sheafification of the constant presheaf
A\in{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg)
,
U\mapsto A
.

Recall that
\mathbb Z\in\DBinAlg
is the initial object; this way we see that
\underline{\mathbb Z}\in\DBinAlg(X)
(being the sheafification of the initial presheaf
\mathbb Z\in{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg)
) is the initial object of
\DBinAlg(X)
. By
4.24
the underlying
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
-valued sheaf of
\underline{\mathbb Z}
is the constant sheaf.

If
X
is also locally weakly contractible the pushforward of
\underline{\mathbb Z}\in\DBinAlg(X)
under
p_{X}
can be described in terms of
2.58
.

Lemma 4.27
.

Assume that
X
satisfies (
4.11
) and is locally weakly contractible. Then there is a natural equivalence of derived binomial rings

C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)\simeq Rp_{X*,\mathrm{Bin}}\underline{\mathbb Z}\in\DBinAlg.

Proof.

We follow the proof in
[
Cla21
, Theorem 12]
replacing
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
with
\DBinAlg
. We have a map of presheaves of binomial rings
\mathbb Z\rightarrow C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(-,\mathbb Z)
; since
X
is weakly contractible this map is an isomorphism on stalks and thus induces an equivalence after sheafification. By
[
DI04
, Theorem 1.3]
the presheaf
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(-,\mathbb Z)\colon U\mapsto C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(U,\mathbb Z)
is in fact an
(\infty,1)
-sheaf; consequently, we get

Rp_{X*,\mathrm{Bin}}\underline{\mathbb Z}\simeq R\Gamma(X,\underline{\mathbb Z})\simeq C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X,\mathbb Z)\in\DBinAlg.

∎

We end the subsection with the sheafy version of
2.63
. Note that the forgetful functor
(-)^{\circ}\colon\DBinAlg\rightarrow\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z)
(see
2.62
) commutes with limits and induces a functor

(-)^{\circ}\colon\DBinAlg(X)\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z))

between the corresponding categories of sheaves.

Lemma 4.28
.

The forgetful functor
(-)^{\circ}\colon\DBinAlg(X)\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z))
commutes will all small colimits.

Proof.

Indeed, the induced functor
(-)^{\circ}\colon{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DBinAlg)\rightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z))
commutes will all small colimits, since the colimits in the category of presheaves are computed pointwise. The statement for sheaves then follows since the sheafification functors also commutes with colimits.
∎

Notation 4.29
.

We will denote the pushout of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{C}
along
\mathcal{B}
in the category
\DBinAlg(X)
by
\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{C}
.

Remark 4.30
.

Similarly to the argument in
4.24
one shows that the forgetful functor

(-)^{\circ}\colon\DBinAlg(X)\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\AAlg_{\mathbb E_{\infty}}\!(\mathbb Z))

(as well as the similar forgetful functor to
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\DAlg(\mathbb Z))
) commutes with push-forwards and pull-backs.

4.3
Functor
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}

In this section we define and study the functor
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in^{\mathrm{coaug}}_{X}
that takes as an input not just a sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
, but rather a pointed (=coaugmented) sheaf
\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
.

Let
X
be a topological space satisfying
4.11
. Let us denote by
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))_{\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}
the under category with respect to the constant sheaf
\underline{\mathbb Z}
. Further, we will call an object of this category
a pointed sheaf
. By
4.26
the constant sheaf
\underline{\mathbb Z}
has a structure of derived binomial ring that makes it an initial object of
\DBinAlg(X)
; thus there is a natural equivalence

\DBinAlg(X)\simeq\DBinAlg(X)_{\setminus\mathbb Z}.

We can define the corresponding forgetful functor

U_{X,\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}\colon\DBinAlg(X)\simeq\DBinAlg(X)_{\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))_{\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}.

Construction 4.31

(Functor
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}
)
.

Note that the homomorphism of derived binomial rings

\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)\simeq\Map_{\mathrm{poly}}(\mathbb Z,\mathbb Z)\overset{\mathrm{ev_{1}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb Z;\,\,\,\,f\mapsto f(1)

by sheafification gives rise to a map

\ev_{1}\colon\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\underline{\mathbb Z})\rightarrow\underline{\mathbb Z}

in
\DBinAlg(X)
.
Let us define a functor

\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}\colon{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))_{\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}\rightarrow\DBinAlg(X)

which maps a pointed sheaf
s\colon\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
to the pushout

\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(s)\coloneqq\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\mathcal{F})\otimes_{\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\underline{\mathbb Z})}\underline{\mathbb Z}

of the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\mathcal{F})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\underline{\mathbb Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{B}in_{X}(s)}
\scriptstyle{\ev_{1}}
\textstyle{\underline{\mathbb Z}}

(4.3)

in
\DBinAlg(X)
.

For
\mathcal{A}\in\DBinAlg(X)
let
\mathbb I_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
denote the unique up to homotopy map (see
4.26
).

Theorem 4.32
.

Functor
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}
is left-adjoint to the forgetful functor

U_{X,\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}\colon\DBinAlg(X)\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))_{\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}.

Proof.

Note that for
(s_{1}\colon\mathbb Z\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{1}),(s_{2}\colon\mathbb Z\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{2})\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))_{\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}
the space of maps
\Map(s_{1},s_{2})
is canonically identified with the homotopy fiber product

\Map(s_{1},s_{2})\simeq\Map(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})\times_{\Map(\underline{\mathbb Z},\mathcal{F}_{2})}\{s_{2}\},

where the map of spaces
\Map(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})\rightarrow\Map(\underline{\mathbb Z},\mathcal{F}_{2})
is induced by pre-composition with
s_{1}
. On the other hand, for
\mathcal{A}\in\DBinAlg(X)
and
(s\colon\mathbb Z\rightarrow\mathcal{F})\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))_{\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}
, we have a canonical equivalence

\Map(\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(s),\mathcal{A})\simeq\Map(\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\mathcal{F}),\mathcal{A})\times_{\Map(\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\underline{\mathbb Z}),\mathcal{A})}\Map(\underline{\mathbb Z},\mathcal{A})

with the maps in the fiber product given by pre-composition with
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(s)
and
\ev_{1}
. Note that
\Map(\underline{\mathbb Z},\mathcal{A})\simeq\{*\}
by
4.26
, and that the map of spaces
\Map(\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\mathcal{F}),\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\Map(\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\underline{\mathbb Z}),\mathcal{A})
can be identified with the map

\Map(\mathcal{F},U_{X}(\mathcal{A}))\rightarrow\Map(\underline{\mathbb Z},U_{X}(\mathcal{A}))

via the
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}\dashv U_{X}
adjunction. Moreover, the map
\mathrm{pt}\simeq\Map(\underline{\mathbb Z},\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\Map(\underline{\mathbb Z},U_{X}(\mathcal{A}))
is given exactly by the canonical map
\mathbb I_{A}\colon\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow U_{X}(\mathcal{A})
. This way we get a functorial in
s
and
\mathcal{A}
equivalence

\Map(\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(s),\mathcal{A})\simeq\Map(F,U_{X}(\mathcal{A}))\times_{\Map(\underline{\mathbb Z},U_{X}(\mathcal{A}))}\{\mathbb I_{A}\}\simeq\Map(s,U_{X,\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}(\mathcal{A})).

∎

Remark 4.33
.

Let
X,Y
be topological spaces satisfying
4.11
and let
f\colon Y\to X
be a continuous map. Then
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in^{\mathrm{coaug}}_{-}
commutes with the inverse image functors
f^{*}
: namely, there is a natural equivalence of functors

f^{*}_{\mathrm{Bin}}\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in^{\mathrm{coaug}}_{X}(-)\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in^{\mathrm{coaug}}_{Y}(f^{*}(-)).

Indeed, by
4.24

f^{*}_{\mathrm{Bin}}
and
f^{*}
intertwine the diagram
Equation

4.3
for
X
and
Y
, and also commute with colimits.

Example 4.34
.

Let
(s\colon\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{F})\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))_{\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}
be a pointed sheaf on
X
and suppose we have fixed a splitting
t\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\underline{\mathbb Z}
. Then
t
induces an equivalence

\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(s)\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\overline{\mathcal{F}}),

where
\overline{\mathcal{F}}\coloneqq\cofib(s)
. Indeed,
t
induces an equivalence
\mathcal{F}\simeq\underline{\mathbb Z}\oplus\overline{\mathcal{F}}
, with
s
identified with the embedding of the first summand. Since
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}
commutes with colimits, we get an equivalence
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\overline{\mathcal{F}})\otimes\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\underline{\mathbb Z})
and then

\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(s)\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\overline{\mathcal{F}})\otimes\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\underline{\mathbb Z})\otimes_{\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\underline{\mathbb Z})}\underline{\mathbb Z}\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\overline{\mathcal{F}}).

4.4
Cohomology of the Kato-Nakayama space

We return to the setup of
Section

4.1
. Namely, let
(X,\mathcal{M}\overset{\alpha}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{X})
be an fs log complex analytic space and
\pi\colon X^{\log}\rightarrow X
be the associated Kato-Nakayama space. We also consider a complex
\mathpzc{exp(\alpha)}\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
defined as the fiber of the map

\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{\exp_{\mathcal{M}}}\mathcal{M}^{\gr}.

Now, we would like to relate
\eexp(\alpha)
and
R\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
. Let us denote by
C(\mathbb R)\colon U\mapsto\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(U,\mathbb R)
and
C(S^{1})\colon U\mapsto\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(U,S^{1})
the sheaves of
\mathbb R
-valued and
S^{1}
-valued continuous functions correspondingly. Note that the universal cover
\mathbb R\rightarrow S^{1}
(given by mapping
x\in\mathbb R
to
e^{2\pi ix}\in S^{1}\subset\mathbb C
) induces a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow C(\mathbb R)\overset{\exp}{\longrightarrow}C(S^{1})\rightarrow 0

(4.4)

of sheaves of abelian groups on
X^{\log}
.

In the case of the Kato-Nakayama space
X^{\log}
we can further apply
R\pi_{*}
to
Equation

4.4
, this way getting a fiber sequence

R\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow R\pi_{*}C(\mathbb R)\xrightarrow{\exp}R\pi_{*}C(S^{1})

in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))
. One then also has a natural map of complexes

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exp_{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{*}C(\mathbb R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exp}
\textstyle{\pi_{*}C(S^{1})}

(4.5)

defined by the formulas

f\mapsto 2\pi i\cdot\mathrm{Re}(\pi^{*}(f)));\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\mathrm{m}\mapsto\mathrm{arg(m)}
20
20

20

Here
\mathrm{Re}
the real part of a complex-valued function; also see
4.3
for definition of
\arg(m)\in\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(X^{\log},S^{1})
.
,

where
f\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
,
\mathrm{m}\in\mathcal{M}^{\gr}(U)
and
U\subset X
. On the other hand, we have the natural transformation
\pi_{*}\to R\pi_{*}
. So we also get a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\exp_{\mathcal{M}}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R\pi_{*}C(\mathbb R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exp}
\textstyle{R\pi_{*}C(S^{1})}

(4.6)

which induces a map

c\colon\eexp(\alpha)\simeq\fib(\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{\exp_{\mathcal{M}}}\mathcal{M}^{\gr}){\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}R\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}.

(4.7)

Remark 4.35
.

Note that since
\eexp(\alpha)
only has non-trivial 0-th and 1-st cohomology sheaves, the map
\eexp(\alpha)\rightarrow R\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
factors through
R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}\coloneqq\tau^{\leq 1}(R\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z})
. Also, the sheaf
C(\mathbb R)
is soft and so
R^{>0}\pi_{*}C(\mathbb R)\simeq 0
, and this way
R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
can be represented by the 2-term complex

\pi_{*}C(\mathbb R)\rightarrow\pi_{*}C(S^{1})

placed in cohomological degrees 0 and 1.

The following lemma has been proven among other things in
[
Shu22
]
(see also
[
AO19
, proof of Theorem 4.2.2]
, or
[
Ste95
, Lemma 2.7]
in the case of normal crossings divisor). We include the proof for reader’s convenience.

Lemma 4.36

(
[
Shu22
, Lemma 5.5]
)
.

Map
Equation

4.7
induces a quasi-isomorphism

\eexp(\alpha)\simeq R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}.

Proof.

By
4.4
the map
\pi
is proper with connected fibers, which allows us to identify
\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
with
\underline{\mathbb Z}
, and it is easy to see that the map
Equation

4.7
induces an equivalence on
H^{0}
. Note also that
H^{1}(\eexp(\alpha))=\coker(\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}^{\gr})=\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}}
; it thus suffices to check that the induced morphism
\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathbb Z
is an isomorphism.

The latter is enough to do on stalks at all points
x\in X
. By proper base change

(R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z})_{x}\simeq\tau^{\leq 1}R\Gamma(\pi^{-1}(x),\underline{\mathbb Z}),

where
\pi^{-1}(x)\simeq\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}},S^{1})
. Note that similarly to the
4.35
we can identify
H^{1}(\pi^{-1}(x),\underline{\mathbb Z})
with the cokernel of the map

\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(\pi^{-1}(x),\mathbb R)\rightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(\pi^{-1}(x),S^{1}).

Tracing through the definition of the map
m\mapsto\arg(m)
one sees that the corresponding map
\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}_{x}}\rightarrow H^{1}(\pi^{-1}(x),\underline{\mathbb Z})
can be represented by the diagram

\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}_{x}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(\pi^{-1}(x),\mathbb R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(\pi^{-1}(x),S^{1})}

where (via the identification
\pi^{-1}(x)\simeq\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}_{x}},S^{1})
) the map

\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}_{x}}\rightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(\pi^{-1}(x),S^{1})

is given by sending
m
to the
“evaluation at
m
map”

\ev_{m}\colon\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}_{x}},S^{1})\rightarrow S^{1}
,
\varphi\mapsto\varphi(m)
. By
4.37
below this map induces an isomorphism

\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}_{x}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\coker(\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(\pi^{-1}(x),\mathbb R)\rightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(\pi^{-1}(x),S^{1}))\simeq H^{1}(\pi^{-1}(x),\underline{\mathbb Z}).

∎

Remark 4.37
.

Let
L\in\mathrm{Lat}
be a lattice, and let
\mathbb T_{L^{\vee}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Hom}(L,S^{1})
be the compact real torus given by
S^{1}
-valued characters of
L
. Then the composite identification

\coker(\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathbb T_{L^{\vee}},\mathbb R)\rightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathbb T_{L^{\vee}},S^{1}))\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}(\mathbb T_{L^{\vee}},\underline{\mathbb Z})\simeq H^{1}_{\mathrm{sing}}(\mathbb T_{L^{\vee}},\mathbb Z)\simeq L

(with the first isomorphism coming from the short exact sequence
Equation

4.4
) is in fact given by the map

g\colon\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathbb T_{L^{\vee}},S^{1})\rightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{sing}}(\mathbb T_{L^{\vee}},\mathbb Z)

that sends
\varphi\in\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathbb T_{L^{\vee}},S^{1})
to the pull-back
\varphi^{*}[S^{1}]\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{sing}}(\mathbb T_{L^{\vee}},\mathbb Z)
of the fundamental class
[S^{1}]\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{sing}}(S^{1},\mathbb Z)
.

Note that we have a map
L\rightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathbb T_{L^{\vee}},S^{1})
sending
\ell\in L
to the evaluation map

\ev_{\ell}\colon\mathrm{Hom}(L,S^{1})\rightarrow S^{1},\quad f\mapsto f(\ell).

Then its composition with
g
is the identity: indeed, we have a natural identification

H_{1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\mathbb T_{L^{\vee}},\mathbb Z)\simeq L^{\vee}

with
\lambda\in L^{\vee}
producing a map
f_{\lambda}\colon S^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb T_{L^{\vee}}
, and the class
\ev_{\ell}^{*}[S^{1}]
pairs with the 1-cycle given by
f_{\lambda}(S^{1})
as
\lambda(\ell)\in\mathbb Z
.

Now it remains to show that in the above situation
R\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
can be reconstructed from
R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
(coaugmented via the natural equivalence
\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}\simeq\underline{\mathbb Z}
) by applying
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}
. The following can be considered as a (partial) generalization of Proposition
3.6
in the relative setting.

Theorem 4.38
.

Let
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a map of Hausdorff topological spaces satisfying
4.11
. Suppose that

1)

\pi
is proper;

2)

for any
x\in X
the fiber
\pi^{-1}(x)\hookrightarrow Y
is a compact finite dimensional real torus
21
21

21

Here the dimension of torus can vary with the point.
.

Consider
R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))_{\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}
with the pointing
\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
given by
\underline{\mathbb Z}\simeq\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
.
Then there is a natural quasi-isomorphism of sheaves of derived binomial rings

\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z})\xrightarrow{\sim}R\pi_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}\underline{\mathbb Z}

Proof.

We have a map

(\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}){\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow R\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z})\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z))_{\setminus\underline{\mathbb Z}}.

By
4.32
this induces a map

\beta\colon\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z})\rightarrow R\pi_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}\underline{\mathbb Z}

in
\DBinAlg(X)
, and it is enough to check that it is an equivalence on fibers at all points of the underlying complexes.

Let
x\in X
be a point, and let
i_{x}\colon\mathrm{pt}\rightarrow X
be the corresponding embedding. Also let
\pi_{x}\colon\pi^{-1}(x)\rightarrow x
be the corresponding projection. Note that
i_{x}^{*}
is
t
-exact. Applying proper base change and
4.33
we see that the restriction of
\beta
to
x
can be identified with the analogous map

\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(R^{\leq 1}\pi_{x,*}\underline{\mathbb Z})\rightarrow R\pi_{x*,\mathrm{Bin}}\underline{\mathbb Z}

(4.8)

for
\pi_{x}\colon\pi^{-1}(x)\rightarrow\mathrm{pt}
. Thus we can reduce to the case of the projection
p_{\mathbb T}\colon\mathbb T\rightarrow\mathrm{pt}
of a compact real torus to the point. Picking a point
t\in\mathbb T
gives a splitting
R\Gamma^{\leq 1}(\mathbb T,\underline{\mathbb Z})\simeq C^{\leq 1}_{{\mathrm{sing}}}(\mathbb T,\mathbb Z)\simeq\mathbb Z\oplus H^{1}_{\mathrm{sing}}(\mathbb T,\mathbb Z)[-1]
, and also identifies
\mathbb T
with
K(H_{1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\mathbb T,\mathbb Z)[1])
. Then via
4.34
, the map
Equation

4.8
is identified with the equivalence

\mathbf{L}\mathrm{Bin}(M^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\sim}C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(K(M),\mathbb Z)

from
3.6
in the case
M=H_{1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\mathbb T,\mathbb Z)[1]
.
∎

Example 4.39
.

Assume that the first truncation
R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
splits as
\underline{\mathbb Z}\oplus(R^{1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z})[-1]
and let
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}\coloneqq R^{1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
. Then in this case by
4.34
we also get a (functorial in the splitting) equivalence

R\pi_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}\underline{\mathbb Z}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\mathcal{L}_{\pi}[-1]).

Moreover, one sees that the natural filtration by
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in^{\leq*}_{X}(\mathcal{L}_{\pi}[-1])
(obtained by sheafification) coincides with the Postnikov filtration: indeed, the
n
-th associated graded piece is given by (the defined analogously)
\mathbf{L}\Gamma_{X}^{n}(\mathcal{L}_{\pi}[-1])\simeq(\wedge^{n}\mathcal{L}_{\pi})[-n]
, which is concentrated in a single cohomological degree
n
. One can see from this that the way different
R^{i}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
’s are glued together inside
R\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
is controlled by how functors
\Gamma^{i}
are glued together inside
\mathrm{Bin}
. We plan to study this relation in more detail in the sequel.

Finally, we get the formula
Equation

1.5
for
\pi\colon X^{\log}\rightarrow X
as a consequence of
4.36
and
4.38
:

Theorem 4.40
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M})
be an fs log analytic space over
\mathbb C
. Let
\pi\colon X^{\log}\rightarrow X
be the natural map from the Kato-Nakayama space back to
X
. Let
\eexp(\alpha)=\fib(\exp_{\mathcal{M}}\colon\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\gr})
, as in
4.7
