. Then there is a natural equivalence of sheaves of derived binomial rings

R\pi_{*,\mathrm{Bin}}\underline{\mathbb Z}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(\eexp(\alpha)).

Consequently, we also get a closed formula for the log Betti cohomology of
X
:

R\Gamma^{\log}_{\mathrm{Betti}}(X,\mathbb Z)\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma(X,\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(\eexp(\alpha))).

Remark 4.41
.

Via
4.30
we also get formulas for the pushforward
R\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
in the categories of sheaves of
\mathbb E_{\infty}
-algebras or derived commutative algebras as the corresponding underlying object of
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in^{\mathrm{coaug}}_{X}(\eexp(\alpha))
.

4.5
Cosheaf of spaces
[X^{\log},\pi]
and its properties

Notation 4.42
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-category with small colimits and let
X
be a topological space. We denote by
{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\coloneqq(\mathrm{h}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{op}}))^{\mathrm{op}}
the category of
\mathscr{C}
-valued hyper-cosheaves on
X
. By a similar argument to
[
Vol21
, Lemma 2.21]
one gets that for cocomplete
\mathscr{C}
the left Kan extension along
\mathrm{Op}(X)\rightarrow\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathrm{Op}(X)^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})\xrightarrow{}\mathrm{h}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
identifies
{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\subset\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathrm{Op}(X)^{{\mathrm{op}}},\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{{\mathrm{op}}}\simeq\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathrm{Op}(X),\mathscr{C})
with

{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\simeq\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}^{\mathrm{L}}(\mathrm{h}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}),\mathscr{C})

(where
\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}^{\mathrm{L}}
denotes the subcategory of small colimit preserving functors). In particular, by
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.5.3.8]
if
\mathscr{C}
is presentable, then so is
{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})
. Via the adjoint functor theorem we get a right adjoint
R\colon{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\rightarrow{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})
to the embedding
{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\subset{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathscr{C})\coloneqq\mathscr{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathrm{Op}(X),\mathscr{C})
, which we will call
hypercosheafification
. As usual one has an explicit formula for it: namely

R(\mathcal{F})(U)\simeq\lim_{V_{\bullet}\rightarrow U}|\mathcal{F}(V_{\bullet})|\in\mathscr{C}

where the limit is taken over all hypercovers
V_{\bullet}\rightarrow U
, and
|\mathcal{F}(V_{\bullet})|
denotes geometric realization of the simplicial object
\mathcal{F}(V_{\bullet})
.

We will be primarily interested in the cases where
\mathscr{C}
is given by
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
,
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
,
\Stk_{/\mathbb Z}
or
\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{/\mathbb Z})
. We have natural adjoint functors

C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(-,\mathbb Z)\colon{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})\rightarrow{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}),\qquad K\colon{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})\rightarrow{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})

and also the functor

K\colon{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{/\mathbb Z}))\rightarrow{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\Stk_{/\mathbb Z})

induced by the functors in Constructions
3.1
and
3.18
correspondingly. Note that
C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(-,\mathbb Z)
can be computed on the level of copresheaves since
C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(-,\mathbb Z)\colon{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}\rightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)
commutes with colimits, but
K
needs to be cosheafified. We also have functors

\iota\colon{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})\rightarrow{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\Stk_{/\mathbb Z}),\qquad\iota\colon{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})\rightarrow{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{/\mathbb Z}))

which are induced by the colimit preserving functor
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}\rightarrow\Stk_{/\mathbb Z}
given by constant sheaf, and the identification
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\simeq\mathrm{Ab}({\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
.

Construction 4.43
.

Let
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a map of topological spaces. Then to
\pi
we can associate a cosheaf
[Y,\pi]\in{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
by putting
[Y,\pi](U)\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
for
U\in\mathrm{Op}(U)
to be
22
22

22

More precisely, this means that we take the geometric realization
|\mathrm{Sing}(\pi^{-1}(U))_{\bullet}|
of the simplicial set of continuous simplices of
\pi^{-1}(U)
.

\pi^{-1}(U)\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
; by
[
DI04
]
this indeed is a hyper-cosheaf. We will occasionally denote the corresponding object of
{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
simply by
Y
.

For any
n\geq 0
let us denote by
\tau_{/X,\leq n}([Y,\pi])
(or simply
\tau_{/X,\leq n}Y
) the relative
n
-truncation of
Y
: namely, the hypercosheafification of the copresheaf
U\mapsto\tau_{\leq n}(\pi^{-1}(U))
where
\tau_{\leq n}\colon{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
denotes the
n
-truncation functor (see e.g.
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.5.6.18]
). For any
n\geq 0
we have a natural map

[Y,\pi]\rightarrow\tau_{/X,\leq n}([Y,\pi]).

Example 4.44
.

Assume
X
is locally weakly contractible and consider the identity map
{\mathrm{id}}\colon X\rightarrow X
. Then we claim that the associated object
[X,{\mathrm{id}}]\in{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
is equivalent to the cosheafification of the constant copresheaf given by the point. Indeed, one has
23
23

23

Since
\{*\}
is a terminal object of
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
.
a map of copresheaves
[X,{\mathrm{id}}]\rightarrow\{*\}
which is locally an equivalence since
X
is locally weakly contractible; thus it induces an equivalence after cosheafifying the target. In particular, in this case
[X,{\mathrm{id}}]
is the final object of
{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
.

More generally, one can see that the cosheafification of a constant copresheaf given by (the underlying homotopy type) of some topological space
S
is explicitly given by
[X\times S,p_{X}]
for the projection
p_{X}\colon X\times S\rightarrow X
.

Construction 4.45

(Relative truncated singular homology)
.

Let
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a map of topological spaces and consider the cosheaf

C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\coloneqq C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}([Y,\pi],\mathbb Z)\in{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})

sending
U\mapsto C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi^{-1}(U),\mathbb Z)
.
For each
n\geq 0
let us denote by
C_{\leq n}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\in{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})
the corresponding relative
n
-truncation: namely, the cosheafification of the copresheaf
U\mapsto C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq n}(\pi^{-1}(U),\mathbb Z)
.

For any
\infty\geq m\geq n
there exists a natural map
C_{\leq m}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\rightarrow C_{\leq n}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)
and by adjunction we also have a natural map

[Y,\pi]\rightarrow K(C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)),

which we can also compose with the above one to get compatible maps

[Y,\pi]\rightarrow K(C_{\leq n}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z))

for any
n\geq 0
.

Remark 4.46
.

Let
Y\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
be a space. The natural map of spaces
Y\rightarrow K(C_{*}^{\mathrm{sing}}(Y,\mathbb Z))
induces a map
\tau_{\leq 0}Y\simeq\pi_{0}(Y)\rightarrow K(C_{\leq 0}^{\mathrm{sing}}(Y,\mathbb Z))
by sending a (path-)connected component
Y_{s}\in\pi_{0}(Y)
to the class
[y_{s}]
of any point
y_{s}\in Y_{s}
. Moreover, the induced map
\mathbb Z\langle\pi_{0}(Y)\rangle\rightarrow C_{\leq 0}^{\mathrm{sing}}(Y,\mathbb Z)
is an isomorphism, and so
K(C_{\leq 0}^{\mathrm{sing}}(Y,\mathbb Z))\simeq\pi_{0}(Y)\times\mathbb Z
, where the map
\tau_{\leq 0}Y\rightarrow\pi_{0}(Y)\times\mathbb Z
is identified with the embedding
\pi_{0}(Y)\times\{1\}\hookrightarrow\pi_{0}(Y)\times\mathbb Z
. This way, for any space
Y
we get a commutative square

\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(Y,\mathbb Z))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau_{\leq 0}Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(C_{\leq 0}^{\mathrm{sing}}(Y,\mathbb Z)).}

(4.9)

If now
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
is a continuous map, by cosheafifying the above square for
f^{-1}(U)
we get a commutative square

\textstyle{[Y,\pi]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau_{/X,\leq 0}([Y,\pi])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(C_{\leq 0}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)),}

(4.10)

in
{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
, where we can also identify the bottom horizontal arrow with the embedding

\tau_{/X,\leq 0}([Y,\pi])\times\{1\}\hookrightarrow\tau_{/X,\leq 0}([Y,\pi])\times\mathbb Z.

Lemma 4.47
.

Let
Y\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}
be weakly homotopy equivalent to a finite dimensional compact real torus. Then the square
Equation

4.9
is a fiber square.

Proof.

Let
Y
be homotopy equivalent equivalent to a torus
\mathbb T
.
Picking a point
x\in\mathbb T
we can split
C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 1}(\mathbb T,\mathbb Z)\simeq\mathbb Z\oplus H_{1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\mathbb T,\mathbb Z)[1]
and identify
\mathbb T\simeq K(L,1)
where
L=H_{1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\mathbb T,\mathbb Z)
. Then
Equation

4.9
is identified with

\textstyle{\{1\}\times K(L,1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y\mapsto([x],y)}
\textstyle{\mathbb Z\times K(L,1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{1\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\mapsto[x]}
\textstyle{\mathbb Z,}

which is a fiber square.
∎

Corollary 4.48
.

Let
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a map of topological spaces, and assume that there is a base
\mathcal{B}
of weakly contractible open subsets
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
in
X
such that
\pi^{-1}(U_{i})
is homotopy equivalent to a compact real torus. Then
Equation

4.10
is a fiber square and takes the following form

\textstyle{[Y,\pi]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X\times\{1\},{\mathrm{id}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X\times\mathbb Z,p_{X}].}

Proof.

Denote by
Z
the fiber product of terms in
Equation

4.10
. By the explicit description of hypercosheafification for any
U\in\mathrm{Op}(X)
we get an equivalence

Z(U)\xrightarrow{\sim}\lim_{V_{\bullet}\rightarrow U}|Z^{\prime}(V_{\bullet})|

where the limit runs over all hypercovers
V_{\bullet}\rightarrow U
and for every term
V_{i}\coloneqq\sqcup_{s}\{U_{s}\subset U\}
the space
Z^{\prime}(V_{i})
is defined as the union over
s
of (homotopy) fiber products

\textstyle{Z^{\prime}(U_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 1}(\pi^{-1}(U_{s}),\mathbb Z))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}(\pi^{-1}(U_{s}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 0}(\pi^{-1}(U_{s}),\mathbb Z)).}

By our assumptions, there is a final family of hypercoverings
V_{\bullet}\rightarrow U
where each connected component
U_{s}\subset U
of each term
V_{i}
appearing belongs to
\mathcal{B}
. The map
Y(U)\rightarrow Z(U)
then can be represented by

\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle V_{\bullet}\rightarrow U}}{{V_{i}=\sqcup_{j}U_{s},U_{s}\in\mathcal{B}}}}|Y(V_{\bullet})|\rightarrow\lim_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle V_{\bullet}\rightarrow U}}{{V_{n}=\sqcup U_{s},U_{s}\in\mathcal{B}}}}|Z^{\prime}(V_{\bullet})|.

This map is an equivalence since for each
i
the map

Y(V_{i})\simeq\sqcup_{s}Y(U_{s})\rightarrow\sqcup_{s}Z^{\prime}(U_{s})\simeq Z^{\prime}(V_{i})

is an equivalence by our assumptions on
\mathcal{B}
and
4.47
. Since
\pi^{-1}(U_{i})
is connected for any
U_{i}\in\mathcal{B}
, by a similar argument we also see that
\tau_{/X,\leq 0}Y
is identified with the cosheafification of the constant copresheaf given by
\{*\}
, and so is equivalent to
{\mathrm{id}}\colon X\rightarrow X
by
4.44
. Meanwhile
K(C_{\leq 0}^{\mathrm{sing}}(Y,\mathbb Z))\simeq\mathbb Z\times\tau_{/X,\leq 0}Y\simeq\mathbb Z\times X
and the map
\tau_{/X,\leq 0}Y\rightarrow K(C_{\leq 0}^{\mathrm{sing}}(Y,\mathbb Z))
is identified with embedding
\{1\}\times X\hookrightarrow\mathbb Z\times X
.
∎

Remark 4.49

(Gerbe structure on
Y
)
.

Let
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
be as in
4.48
and let
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}\simeq H_{1}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\in{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})
denote cosheafification of the copresheaf given by
U\mapsto H_{1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi^{-1}(U),\mathbb Z)
. One can think of
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}
as a dual version of
R^{1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
. We have a natural map
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}[1]\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 1}(\pi,\mathbb Z)
, inducing the map
B\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}\rightarrow K(C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 1}(\pi,\mathbb Z))
(here we denoted by
B\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}
the cosheaf of spaces given by
K(\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}[1])
).

The abelian group object structure on
C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 1}(\pi,\mathbb Z)
provides in particular the addition map

K(C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 1}(\pi,\mathbb Z))\times K(C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 1}(\pi,\mathbb Z))\rightarrow K(C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 1}(\pi,\mathbb Z)).

The induced map

B\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}\times K(C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 1}(\pi,\mathbb Z))\rightarrow K(C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 1}(\pi,\mathbb Z))

then preserves the fibers of the projection
K(C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 1}(\pi,\mathbb Z))\rightarrow K(C^{\mathrm{sing}}_{\leq 0}(\pi,\mathbb Z))\simeq[X\times\mathbb Z,p_{X}]
. In particular, it induces an action

B\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}\times[Y,\pi]\rightarrow[Y,\pi]

via
4.48
. Moreover, it endows
[Y,\pi]
with the structure of a torsor over
B\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}
, meaning that on any
U\in\mathcal{B}
it can be identified with the addition map
B\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}(U)\times B\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}(U)\rightarrow B\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}(U)
. This way we can view the cosheaf of spaces
[Y,\pi]\in{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})
represented by
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
as a sort of gerbe over the cosheaf
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}\in{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})
.

Remark 4.50
.

Alternatively, one can also identify
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}[1]\in{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})
with the tensor product
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}\otimes S^{1}
where
S^{1}
is the cosheafification of the constant presheaf given by
S^{1}\in\mathrm{Ab}({\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})\simeq{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
. This way one can view
Y
as a torsor over
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}\otimes S^{1}
(in the sense of
4.49
).

Let us now supply some examples for
4.48
. One of the suitable but also rather general setups is given by
real analytic maps
between
real semi-analytic spaces
. More precisely, consider the following definition:

Definition 4.51
.

We will call a map
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
of topological spaces
locally semi-analytic
if locally on
X
it is homeomorphic to the restriction of a real semi-analytic map
f\colon\mathbb R^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb R^{n}
to some relatively compact semi-analytic subsets
V\subset\mathbb R^{m}
and
U\subset\mathbb R^{n}
(in particular, we want
f(V)\subset U
).

The key result in this context is the following stronger version of base change theorem, for which we were not able to find a reference, so include a proof.

Proposition 4.52
.

Let
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a proper map between topological spaces that is locally semi-analytic in the sense of
4.51
. Then each point
x\in X
has a basis of neighborhoods
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
,
x\in U_{i}
such that the embedding

\pi^{-1}(x)\rightarrow\pi^{-1}(U_{i})

is a homotopy equivalence.

Proof.

First of all, question is local, so we can assume that
\pi
is the restriction of some analytic map from
\pi\colon\mathbb R^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb R^{n}
to some relatively compact (and automatically locally closed) semi-analytic subsets. We then can assume that both
X
and
Y
are Hausdorff, paracompact and locally weakly contractible, since bounded closed subanalytic subsets of
\mathbb R^{n}
admit a triangulation (
[
Har76
]
).
Moreover, the square of the distance function
d(x,-)^{2}\colon\mathbb R^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb R_{\geq 0}\subset\mathbb R
defines an analytic map
\delta\colon X\rightarrow\mathbb R_{\geq 0}
with the property that the preimage
\delta^{-1}(0)
is given exactly by the point
\{x\}\subset X
, and subsets
U_{\varepsilon}\coloneqq\delta^{-1}([0,\varepsilon))\subset X
form a basis of neighborhoods of
x
in
X
. By replacing
X
with a suitable compact neighborhood
24
24

24

For example,
\delta^{-1}([0,t])
for sufficiently small
t>0
.
of
x
, we can assume that the composition of
\pi^{\prime}\coloneqq\delta\circ\pi
is proper, and it would be enough to show that the map

j\colon\pi^{\prime-1}(0)\rightarrow\pi^{\prime-1}([0,\varepsilon))

is a homotopy equivalence for small enough
\varepsilon>0
. For this, by Whitehead’s theorem it is enough to show that
j^{*}
induces an isomorphism on Čech cohomology
H^{1}(-,\underline{G})
for any (not necessarily commutative) group
G
functorially in
G
(this implies that
j
induces on isomorphism on
\pi_{1}(-)
) and, then, that
j^{*}
is an isomorphism on
H^{i}(-,\mathcal{L})
for any local system
\mathcal{L}
. Here we implicitly used that
\pi^{\prime-1}([0,\varepsilon))
is locally weakly contractible to relate Čech to singular cohomology.

The main tool that we will use is Hardt’s theorem (see
[
Tei89
, Théorème de Hardt, p.171]
, which we apply to the graph of
\pi^{\prime}
), which (under our assumptions on
\pi
) guarantees that for small enough
\varepsilon
one can identify the map
\pi^{\prime}
over the open interval
(0,\varepsilon)
with the projection
(0,\varepsilon)\times S\rightarrow(0,\varepsilon)
for some subanalytic set
S
. Now, for the first step, consider sheaves
R^{0}\pi^{\prime}_{*}\underline{G}
and
R^{1}\pi^{\prime}_{*}\underline{G}
(the latter is the sheaf of pointed sets on
[0,\varepsilon)
). Restrictions of both sheaves to
(0,\varepsilon)
are constant, and so their global sections on
[0,\varepsilon)
are given by the fibers at
\{0\}\hookrightarrow[0,\varepsilon)
. By proper base change the latter can be identified with
H^{0}(\pi^{\prime-1}(0),\underline{G})
and
H^{1}(\pi^{\prime-1}(0),\underline{G})
correspondingly. One also sees that
H^{1}(R^{0}\pi^{\prime}_{*}\underline{G})
is a single point, and so global sections
\Gamma([0,\varepsilon),R^{1}\pi^{\prime}_{*}\underline{G})
compute
H^{1}(\pi^{\prime-1}([0,\varepsilon),\underline{G})
. We get that

j^{*}\colon H^{1}(\pi^{\prime-1}([0,\varepsilon)),\underline{G})\rightarrow H^{1}(\pi^{\prime-1}(0),\underline{G})

is an isomorphism for any
G
.

Then, having a local system
\mathcal{L}
on
\pi^{\prime-1}([0,\varepsilon))
it remains to show that the restriction map

j^{*}\colon H^{i}(\pi^{\prime-1}([0,\varepsilon)),\mathcal{L})\rightarrow H^{i}(\pi^{\prime-1}(0),j^{*}\mathcal{L})

is an isomorphism.
By induction on
i
and similar argument as above one can see that sheaves
R^{i}\pi^{\prime}\mathcal{L}
do not have higher cohomology (since their global sections are given by the restriction to
\{0\}
), and that
\Gamma([0,\varepsilon),R^{i}\pi^{\prime}\mathcal{L})
is naturally equivalent to
H^{i}(\pi^{\prime-1}([0,\varepsilon)),\mathcal{L})
; consequently the pull-back
j^{*}
above is an isomorphism for any
i
.
∎

Remark 4.53
.

Let us note that
4.52
in fact holds in a bigger generality of
locally definable maps
: namely
\pi
should satisfy the property in
4.51
with
semi-analytic
replaced by
definable
for any given
o
-minimal model structure. Indeed, the only facts that we used are the existence of triangulation and Hardt’s theorem, that also have analogues in this generality (see e.g.
[
Cos00
, Theorems 4.4 and 5.22]
).

Let us also record the following straightforward observation.

Lemma 4.54
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M})
be an fs log complex analytic space. Then the map
\pi\colon X^{\log}\rightarrow X
is locally semi-analytic in the sense of
4.51
. Consequently,
4.48
applies to
\pi
.

Proof.

For a fine and saturated monoid
P
let
\mathbb A(P)
denote the analytification of the affine toric variety
\Spec\mathbb C[P]
; it has a natural log-structure given by
P\cdot\mathcal{O}^{\times}\subset\mathcal{O}
. Since the log structure on
X
is fs, by
[
Ogu18
, Corollary II.2.3.6]
for any point
x\in X
there exists an open neighborhood
x\in U\subset X
and an fs monoid
P
with the complex analytic map
f\colon U\rightarrow\mathbb A(P)
such that
\mathcal{M}
is obtained as a pull-back of the log-structure on
\mathbb A(P)
. Moreover, one also has a homeomorphism

U^{\log}\simeq\mathbb A(P)^{\log}\times_{\mathbb A(P)}U,

and this way it will be enough to endow
\mathbb A(P)^{\log}\rightarrow{\mathbb A(P)}
with the real analytic structure. By
[
Ogu18
, Proposition V.1.2.7]
this map is identified with
\Map_{\mathrm{Mon}}(P,\mathbb R_{\geq 0}\times S^{1})\rightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{Mon}}(P,\mathbb C)
, where
\mathbb C
is considered as a monoid by multiplication, the sets of maps are endowed with the topology induced by the targets, and the map itself is induced by the homomorphism
\mathbb R_{\geq 0}\times S^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb C
sending
(r,z)
to
r\cdot z
. Let
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
be some generators of
P
; then we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Map_{\mathrm{Mon}}(P,\mathbb R_{\geq 0}\times S^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Map_{\mathrm{Mon}}(P,\mathbb C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathbb R_{\geq 0}\times S^{1})^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb C^{n}}

with vertical arrows induced by evaluations at
p_{i}
’s. Note that each map
\mathbb R_{\geq 0}\times S^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb C
naturally embeds into
\mathbb R\times\mathbb C\rightarrow\mathbb C
,
(t,z)\mapsto t\cdot z
, where the map is clearly real-analytic. It is also easy to see that the preimage of any relatively compact
U\subset\mathbb C
in
\mathbb R_{\geq 0}\times S^{1}
is also relatively compact in
\mathbb R\times\mathbb C
. Moreover the spaces of maps above define semi-analytic subspaces of
(\mathbb R\times\mathbb C)^{n}
and
\mathbb C^{n}
correspondingly (they are cut out by finitely many equations induced by relations between
p_{i}
’s). Thus the restriction of the map
\Map_{\mathrm{Mon}}(P,\mathbb R_{\geq 0}\times S^{1})\rightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{Mon}}(P,\mathbb C)
to the intersection with any relatively compact
U\subset\mathbb C^{n}
is obtained by restriction to some relatively compact semi-analytic subspaces in
(\mathbb R\times\mathbb C)^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb C^{n}
, which is exactly what we wanted to show.
∎

Remark 4.55
.

Let
X
satisfy
4.11
. The functor

\Map_{\DBinAlg}(-,\mathbb Z)\colon\DBinAlg^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}

extends to a functor

[-]_{X}\coloneqq\Map_{\DBinAlg}(-,\mathbb Z)\colon\DBinAlg(X)^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}}).

There is also a natural functor

C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(-/X,\mathbb Z)\colon{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\DBinAlg(X)

with
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(\mathcal{F}/X,\mathbb Z)
sending
U\in\mathrm{Op}(X)
to
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(\mathcal{F}(U),\mathbb Z)
. From
3.14
and descent one can see that
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(-/X,\mathbb Z)
induces a fully faithful embedding when restricted to the subcategory of
{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pc}})^{\mathrm{op}}
spanned by cosheaves whose values locally are connected nilpotent spaces of finite type. In particular, for such cosheaves
\mathcal{F}
the natural map

\mathcal{F}\rightarrow[C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(\mathcal{F}/X,\mathbb Z)]_{X}

is an equivalence. Applying this to
\mathcal{F}=[X^{\log},\pi]
represented by
\pi\colon X^{\log}\rightarrow X
we see from
4.40
that one can reconstruct
[X^{\log},\pi]
from
\eexp(\alpha)
as

[X^{\log},\pi]\xrightarrow{\sim}[\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(\eexp(\alpha))]_{X}.

4.6
Algebraic approximation
X^{\log}_{\mathbb H}
to
[X^{\log},\pi]

Let us finish with our construction of an
“algebraic approximation”

X^{\log}_{\mathbb H}
of
X^{\log}
. The construction in fact applies to any
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
as in
4.48
, but for brevity let us just restrict to the case of Kato-Nakayama space.

Construction 4.56

(A cosheaf of stacks
X^{\log}_{\mathbb H}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})
be a
{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}
-valued cosheaf. Recall the colimit preserving functor
-\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb H\colon{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{/\mathbb Z})
(
3.20
). It induces a functor

-\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb H\colon{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)^{\leq 0})\rightarrow{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{/\mathbb Z})).

Recall also the map
i\colon\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathbb H
in
\mathrm{Ab}(\Stk_{/\mathbb Z})
(
3.21
). For any
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
it induces a commutative diagram

\textstyle{C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\underline{\mathbb Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{id}}\otimes i}
\textstyle{C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb H\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{\leq 0}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{C_{\leq 0}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\underline{\mathbb Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{id}}\otimes i}
\textstyle{C_{\leq 0}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb H}

(4.11)

(here we view
C_{\leq i}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)
as a cosheaf of algebraic stacks via the functor
\iota
from
4.42
).
Recall that for
\pi\colon X^{\log}\rightarrow X
by
4.48
the cosheaf
[X^{\log},\pi]
is equivalent to the fiber product

\textstyle{[X^{\log},\pi]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X,{\mathrm{id}}]\times\{1\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X,{\mathrm{id}}]\times\mathbb Z}

as in
Equation

4.10
, with right vertical arrow identified with
K
applied to
C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\rightarrow C_{\leq 0}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)
. By composing with the outer square of
Equation

4.11
we get a commutative diagram

\textstyle{[X^{\log},\pi]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb H\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X,{\mathrm{id}}]\times\{1\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X,{\mathrm{id}}]\times\mathbb Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X,{\mathrm{id}}]\times\mathbb H;}

(4.12)

here we identified
K(C_{\leq 0}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb H)
with
[X,{\mathrm{id}}]\times\mathbb H
(and we did not distinguish between
\mathbb H
and
K(\mathbb H)
).
We then finally define the cosheaf of stacks
X^{\log}_{\mathbb H}
as the fiber product

\textstyle{X^{\log}_{\mathbb H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X,{\mathrm{id}}]\times\{1\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{id}}\times i}
\textstyle{[X,{\mathrm{id}}]\times\mathbb H.}

Outer commutative square in
Equation

4.12
induces a natural map

i_{X}\colon[X^{\log},\pi]\rightarrow X_{\mathbb H}^{\log}

of cosheaves of algebraic stacks on
X
.

Remark 4.57
.

Similarly to
4.49
one can view
X_{\mathbb H}^{\log}
as a gerbe, but over the cosheaf of group schemes given by
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}\otimes_{{\mathbb Z}}\mathbb H
; more precisely, by construction, it is induced from the
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}
-gerbe given by
X^{\log}
via the map
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\pi}^{\vee}\otimes_{{\mathbb Z}}\mathbb H
induced by
\underline{\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\mathbb H
. Note also that
\mathcal{L}_{\pi}
in this case is identified with
\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\gr}}
.

Finally, we would like to reformulate
4.40
in terms of the map
i_{X}
.

Construction 4.58

(
\mathcal{O}
-cohomology of a cosheaf of stacks)
.

Recall the functor

\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}\colon\Stk^{\mathrm{op}}_{/\mathbb Z}\rightarrow\DAlg(\mathbb Z)

from
3.17
. It is limit commuting, and thus induces a functor

\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}\colon{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\Stk_{/\mathbb Z})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{h}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,{\mathscr{D}}(\mathbb Z)).

Example 4.59
.

Let
\pi\colon Y\rightarrow X
be a continuous map and assume that
Y
is locally weakly contractible. Let
\iota([Y,\pi])\in{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{C}\mathrm{o}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{hv}}(X,\Stk_{/\mathbb Z})
be the cosheaf of stacks represented by
Y
. Then

\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(\iota([Y,\pi]))\simeq R\pi_{\DAlg,*}\underline{\mathbb Z},

where pushforward is taken in the category of
\DAlg(\mathbb Z)
-valued hypersheaves. Indeed, the sections on a given open
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(\iota([Y,\pi]))(U)\in\DAlg(\mathbb Z)
are naturally identified with
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi^{-1}(U),\mathbb Z)
, while sections of
R\pi_{\DAlg,*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
are naturally identified with the Čech cohomology
R\Gamma(\pi^{-1}(U),\underline{\mathbb Z})
. One has a functorial in
U
map
R\Gamma(\pi^{-1}(U),\underline{\mathbb Z})\rightarrow C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi^{-1}(U),\mathbb Z)
which is an equivalence by
4.27
.

Example 4.60
.

Let us consider the cosheafification of the constant copresheaf on
X
given by
K(\mathbb H)
: it is naturally equivalent to
[X,{\mathrm{id}}]\times\mathbb H
. Assume
X
is locally weakly contractible. Then
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(X\times\mathbb H)
is described as the sheafification of the constant presheaf given by
\mathrm{Bin}(\mathbb Z)
, or, in other words, the sheaf of derived commutative algebras that underlies
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\underline{\mathbb Z})
.

Lemma 4.61
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M})
be an fs log complex analytic space. Then the map

i_{X}\colon[X^{\log},\pi]\rightarrow X^{\log}_{\mathbb H}

of cosheaves of stacks on
X
induces an equivalence

i_{X}^{*}\colon\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(X^{\log}_{\mathbb H})\rightarrow\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}([X^{\log},\pi]),

of
\DAlg(\mathbb Z)
-valued sheaves on
X
, which is explicitly given by

\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z})\xrightarrow{\sim}R\pi_{\DAlg,*}\underline{\mathbb Z}.

Proof.

First of all, by
4.59
we can identify
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(X^{\log})
and
R\pi_{\DAlg,*}\underline{\mathbb Z}
. Moreover, via
3.22

\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(K(C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb H))
can be naturally identified with (the underlying sheaf of derived commutative algebras of)
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z})
, while
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(X\times\mathbb H)\simeq\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\underline{\mathbb Z})
. Moreover,
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(X\times\{1\})\simeq\underline{\mathbb Z}
and the map
\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(X\times\mathbb H)\rightarrow\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(X\times\{1\})
is given exactly by the
“evaluation at 1”

\ev_{1}\colon\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\underline{\mathbb Z})\rightarrow\underline{\mathbb Z}
. This way we obtain the map

\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z})\coloneqq\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z})\otimes_{\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}(\underline{\mathbb Z})}\underline{\mathbb Z}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}R\pi_{\DAlg,*}\underline{\mathbb Z}

via the map of cosheaves

[X^{\log},\pi]\rightarrow X^{\log}_{\mathbb H}\coloneqq K(C_{\leq 1}^{\mathrm{sing}}(\pi,\mathbb Z)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb H)\times_{(X\times\mathbb H)}(X\times\{1\}).

We know that this map is an equivalence by
4.40
. It thus remains to show that the natural map
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z})\rightarrow\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(X^{\log}_{\mathbb H})
is also an equivalence. By
4.54
there is a base of opens
\mathcal{B}
, where for any
U\in\mathcal{B}
the preimage
\pi^{-1}(U)
is homotopy equivalent to a torus. On such
U
the map
\mathbf{L}\mathcal{B}in_{X}^{\mathrm{coaug}}(R^{\leq 1}\pi_{*}\underline{\mathbb Z}(U))\rightarrow\mathscr{O}_{\DAlg}(X^{\log}_{\mathbb H}(U))
is an equivalence by
3.6
; it then follows that the map is an equivalence in general by passing to hypersheafification.
∎

.

In fact, via a version
25

\DAlg(R)^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\Stk_{\!/\!R}

gives a fully faithful embedding of a certain natural subcategory of commutative derived rings.
of the theory of affine stacks of Toën
[
Toë06
]
one can view
X^{\log}_{\mathbb H}
as the
“affinization”
of
